# Surface changes — Phase 15 (SDK Ergonomics) and Phase 15.1 (Ergonomics Gap Closure)

<!--
  GENERATED — do not edit this file by hand.
  Assembled from docs/surface-changes/15*-*.md by
  tools/assemble_surface_changes.py. To change anything here, edit the
  owning fragment and re-run `make surface-changes`.
-->

## What this document is

This is the **migration note D-15B promises**. D-15B took free rein on the public API pre-1.0 on the explicit understanding that anyone building against v0.1 or v0.2 gets a migration; this is it. Every surface Phase 15 added, reshaped, removed, or changed the behavior of is recorded below with the reason it moved and what an adopter has to do about it.

It is assembled from the per-plan fragments under `docs/surface-changes/`, which each plan wrote **as it landed**. A migration note written retroactively at phase close is a note written from the diff, which is the one thing it is supposed to add information to.

**The `Behavior changes` section is why this document exists at all.** `docs/surface/v0.2-surface.json` is generated from every module's `__all__` and records signatures; it is structurally incapable of seeing a change like D-02's, where `load_artifact` kept its signature and changed what it returns for a timestamp-shaped scalar. A generated diff and a curated log are two halves of one guarantee, and `tests/test_surface_completeness.py` asserts they agree.

**Phase 16 consumes this document directly.** The documentation rewrite describes the surface that exists; this is the record of how it got there from the surface Phase 14 documented.

## How to read an entry

Every entry carries seven fields: `Surface`, `Public surface`, `Change type`, `Before`, `After`, `Why`, and `Migration`. The assembler refuses to build this document from an entry missing any of them.

`Public surface: true` means the entry names an exported Python name and appears in the generated manifest diff. `Public surface: false` entries are real changes an adopter must act on that are **not** Python API — lint scope, tooling, committed schemas, and artifact *document formats*. They are grouped separately under each change type so a Python API change is never mistaken for a YAML reshape. The classification is checked in both directions by `tests/test_surface_completeness.py`: a public change mislabelled non-public fails, and a non-public entry whose name turns up in the manifest diff fails too.

**400 entries**, of which **181** name a public Python surface.

## Added

Surfaces that did not exist at phase start. Nothing here breaks an existing integration; every entry is a path that is now available.

159 entries — 131 public Python surface, 28 not public Python API.

### Public Python surface

#### ccf.deployment.classes.resolve_artifact_class

- Surface: ccf.deployment.classes.resolve_artifact_class
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — `ccf.controlplane.artifacts` has no registry of artifact
  classes. Its `ArtifactClass` values are module-level constants that callers
  import by name, which is the right shape for a caller who knows statically
  which class it wants and the wrong shape for a manifest, whose whole point is
  that it does not
- After: `resolve_artifact_class(class_id: str) -> ArtifactClass`, returning the
  SDK's own constant for an id the SDK owns and
  `declared_artifact_class(class_id)` for every other id
- Why: the manifest declares each artifact by `path, key, class`, where `class`
  is a string. Nothing in the tree turned that string into a contract. The
  fallthrough is the D-01 line made operational: the SDK owns the schema of what
  it ships and never invents one for what it does not, so an unrecognized id is a
  deployment-authored schema and gets the open `DeclaredArtifact` envelope — by
  design, not by failure. SDK ids resolve from the reviewed mapping *first*, so a
  manifest cannot shadow an SDK class into the permissive envelope
- Migration: none — new function. Callers who import an `ArtifactClass` constant
  by name may keep doing so; this is the additional string-keyed direction, not a
  replacement

*Recorded by `15-06-class-lookup.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.classes.SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES

- Surface: ccf.deployment.classes.SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — there was no enumeration of the artifact classes the SDK owns
- After: `dict[str, ArtifactClass]` keyed by `class_id`, today
  `ccf.half-life-table` and `ccf.debt-profile`
- Why: exported rather than private because "which artifact classes does this SDK
  ship?" is a question an adopter writing a manifest actually asks, and answering
  it by reading source is worse than answering it by import. It is hand-written
  by name rather than scanned out of `globals()` so a new SDK class is a
  deliberate reviewed addition rather than something that appears by import
  accident, and `tests/deployment/test_class_lookup.py` holds it to the artifacts
  module so the hand-maintenance cannot silently fall behind
- Migration: none — new constant. Treat it as read-only; mutating it changes
  resolution globally for the process

*Recorded by `15-06-class-lookup.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.resolve_artifact_class

- Surface: ccf.deployment.resolve_artifact_class
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — `ccf.deployment.__all__` was `[]` and the package exported
  nothing at all
- After: re-exported from the package root, so the adopter-facing import is
  `from ccf.deployment import resolve_artifact_class`
- Why: the package root is the adopter's entry point to the wiring surface; a
  helper reachable only at its defining module makes the submodule layout part of
  the public contract
- Migration: none

*Recorded by `15-06-class-lookup.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES

- Surface: ccf.deployment.SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the package exported nothing
- After: re-exported from the package root alongside `resolve_artifact_class`
- Why: same reason as the function — the two are read together, and splitting
  them across import paths would be an arbitrary seam
- Migration: none

*Recorded by `15-06-class-lookup.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifact_base.VersionedArtifact

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifact_base.VersionedArtifact
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the base existed as `ccf.controlplane.artifacts._VersionedArtifact`,
  module-private, with no module of its own
- After: a public `CCFContract` subclass declaring the single `semver` lane,
  validated through `validate_semver`, with no default
- Why: two reasons, and both are structural. **It had to move**, because
  D-IMPL-77 requires `EntityResolutionSpec` gain `semver` *in place*, so
  `ccf.controlplane.artifacts` must name a class from `ccf.assembly.worker` to
  declare `RESOLUTION_SPEC` — and `worker` reaches `ccf.controlplane.registry`,
  which needs `ArtifactClass`. Leaving these primitives in `artifacts` closed
  `artifacts -> worker -> registry -> artifacts` at **import** time, taking the
  package down rather than tripping a lint. The real closure proved wider than
  the registry edge alone: `worker -> peering.admission -> peering.trust_policy
  -> artifacts` closes it too, so every module importing only the relocated
  primitives now takes them from `artifact_base`. **It had to become public**,
  because L2's `EntityResolutionSpec` now inherits it, and an L2 contract
  inheriting a module-private Control Plane type is a relocation hazard the next
  refactor trips over
- Migration: none — `ccf.controlplane.artifacts` re-exports it, and
  `_VersionedArtifact` survives there as a module-level alias

*Recorded by `15-07-resolution-spec.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifact_base.ArtifactClass

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifact_base.ArtifactClass
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent at this path — defined in `ccf.controlplane.artifacts`
- After: the same frozen dataclass (`class_id`, `contract`, `validate`),
  unchanged in behavior, defined here
- Why: it is the name `ccf.controlplane.registry` needed, and the one whose
  location closed the cycle. `artifact_base` imports nothing from `ccf` except
  `ccf.contracts.base`, and its module docstring states that constraint as the
  property it exists to hold — a future import into it would re-close the cycle
  silently
- Migration: none — `ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ArtifactClass` still resolves

*Recorded by `15-07-resolution-spec.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifact_base.validate_semver

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifact_base.validate_semver
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent at this path — defined in `ccf.controlplane.artifacts`
- After: the same function and the same regex, defined here
- Why: it travels with the base that calls it. The semver grammar has exactly
  one home (E7); re-declaring the regex in a second module so the base could
  move would be the defect this relocation exists to avoid
- Migration: none — `ccf.controlplane.artifacts.validate_semver` still resolves

*Recorded by `15-07-resolution-spec.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.VersionedArtifact

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.VersionedArtifact
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent from `__all__` — the module exposed only the private
  `_VersionedArtifact`
- After: re-exported from `ccf.controlplane.artifact_base` and listed in
  `__all__`
- Why: the re-export is what makes the relocation a non-event for adopters. The
  module's public surface does not shrink, and a caller who reached for the base
  through this module keeps working
- Migration: none

*Recorded by `15-07-resolution-spec.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.RESOLUTION_SPEC

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.RESOLUTION_SPEC
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — there was no artifact class for the L2 resolution
  vocabulary, so a deployment's ontology document could not reach
  `parse_artifact` at all
- After: `ArtifactClass(class_id="ccf.resolution-spec", contract=EntityResolutionSpec)`,
  also registered in `ccf.deployment.classes.SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` so a D-05
  manifest can name it
- Why: this is the phase's highest-duplication target. The spec's field names
  already match the ontology YAML keys exactly, so the ~20-line hand-marshaller
  repeated across nine files was pure wiring — three things blocked its removal
  (no `semver`, the operator-facing attribute maps riding in the same document
  per D-03, and no registered class) and this plan removes all three. Closed-world
  validation comes free: `CCFContract` is frozen and `extra=forbid`, so an
  unknown key fails at parse with no new machinery
- Migration: deployments hand-marshalling an ontology document into an
  `EntityResolutionSpec` replace that code with
  `parse_artifact(document, RESOLUTION_SPEC)`. Note the document handed to
  `parse_artifact` is the **resolution-spec mapping**, not the whole ontology
  file: root-level `schema_version` / `artifact_version` keys are file-level
  decoration and are refused by the closed-world contract, exactly as they
  already are for `ccf.half-life-table`, whose document is the nested
  `half_life_table:` mapping

*Recorded by `15-07-resolution-spec.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.HALF_LIFE_TABLE

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.HALF_LIFE_TABLE
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: defined as a module-level constant but absent from `__all__`
- After: listed in `__all__`
- Why: the constant was always importable and always intended as public — the
  omission was an oversight, and a measurable one. 15-06's anti-drift test had
  to scan public module *attributes* rather than `__all__` precisely because an
  `__all__`-only scan would have found zero `ArtifactClass` instances and passed
  while proving nothing. This plan owns `artifacts.py`, so it is the plan that
  can fix it
- Migration: none — the import path is unchanged; `from ccf.controlplane.artifacts
  import *` now yields it

*Recorded by `15-07-resolution-spec.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DEBT_PROFILE

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DEBT_PROFILE
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: defined as a module-level constant but absent from `__all__`
- After: listed in `__all__`
- Why: same omission and same fix as `HALF_LIFE_TABLE` — with `RESOLUTION_SPEC`

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.OperatorParametersArtifact

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.OperatorParametersArtifact
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — there was no shared type over operator-parameters artifacts
- After: an empty `VersionedArtifact` subclass carrying no fields, the marker
  base of all four category contracts
- Why: so the operator-registry builder can type its `parameters` mapping as
  `Mapping[str, OperatorParametersArtifact]` and accept all four categories *by
  type*. Without it the builder must `isinstance`-filter four unrelated classes,
  and that idiom, copied into adopter code to assemble the mapping, is exactly
  the new duplication class this surface exists to prevent
- Migration: none — new name

*Recorded by `15-08-operator-parameters.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.TraversalNeighborhoodParametersArtifact

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.TraversalNeighborhoodParametersArtifact
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — a deployment's traversal parameters were hand-marshalled into
  `TraversalNeighborhoodParameters(seed_entity_classes=..., relationship_types=...,
  depth_limit=...)` at each call site
- After: `class_id` `ccf.operator-parameters.traversal`; a contract subclassing
  `ccf.operators.params.TraversalNeighborhoodParameters` verbatim plus the
  `semver` lane
- Why: these parameter contracts are already `/src` code whose own module
  docstring calls them "Control Plane *values* that a deployment declares,
  versions, and replays against" — refusing to parse them declaratively was
  inconsistent rather than principled (D-01). Defined here rather than by adding
  `semver` to `params.py` because `ccf.operators` is L4 and importing the
  Control Plane's versioned base there would invert a layer
- Migration: replace the hand-built parameter block with
  `parse_artifact(document, OPERATOR_PARAMETERS_TRAVERSAL)`. A parsed artifact
  **is** a `TraversalNeighborhoodParameters` and passes straight to
  `TraversalNeighborhood(parameters=...)` with no unwrapping

*Recorded by `15-08-operator-parameters.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.TemporalAsOfParametersArtifact

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.TemporalAsOfParametersArtifact
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — `TemporalAsOfParameters(require_valid_at_boundary=...)` built
  by hand at each call site
- After: `class_id` `ccf.operator-parameters.temporal`; a contract subclassing
  `ccf.operators.params.TemporalAsOfParameters` verbatim plus the `semver` lane
- Why: same D-01 argument as the traversal class; the four categories move
  together or the surface is incoherent
- Migration: `parse_artifact(document, OPERATOR_PARAMETERS_TEMPORAL)`; the parsed
  artifact is accepted directly by `TemporalAsOf`

*Recorded by `15-08-operator-parameters.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.SimilaritySelectParametersArtifact

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.SimilaritySelectParametersArtifact
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — `SimilaritySelectParameters(query=..., minimum_score_percent=...,
  ranked_entity_classes=...)` built by hand, with the corpus attribute map read
  separately out of the ontology document
- After: `class_id` `ccf.operator-parameters.similarity`; a contract subclassing
  `ccf.operators.params.SimilaritySelectParameters` verbatim, **plus one
  artifact-only field**, `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class: Mapping[str, str]`
- Why: same D-01 argument, with one deliberate asymmetry. The corpus map governs
  `TokenOverlapIndex` *construction*, not operator invocation, so it is read by
  the registry builder before the operator exists. Adding it to
  `SimilaritySelectParameters` would put a construction-time value on the
  per-invocation L4 invoke contract, so it lives on the artifact instead.
  **`SimilaritySelectParameters` itself is unchanged** and still declares exactly
  `query`, `minimum_score_percent`, `ranked_entity_classes`
- Migration: `parse_artifact(document, OPERATOR_PARAMETERS_SIMILARITY)`; the
  parsed artifact is accepted directly by `SimilaritySelect`, and its
  `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class` is what the index construction reads —
  a deployment carrying that map in its ontology document moves it here

*Recorded by `15-08-operator-parameters.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.PolicyFilterParametersArtifact

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.PolicyFilterParametersArtifact
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — `PolicyFilterParameters(...)` built by hand, with
  `permitted_policy_bundle_versions=frozenset({policy_version})` where
  `policy_version` is the return value of `registry.register(...)`
- After: `class_id` `ccf.operator-parameters.policy`; a contract declaring
  exactly `semver`, `classification_attribute_by_class` and
  `permitted_classifications`. It is **not** a `PolicyFilterParameters` subclass
  and declares **no** `permitted_policy_bundle_versions`
- Why: per D-IMPL-76 the admitted policy-bundle version set is a content hash
  minted at registration, so no authored artifact can state it — the adopter
  passes the pin set to the operator-registry builder. Subclassing and leaving
  the field required means the artifact can never parse; subclassing and
  defaulting it to `frozenset()` means a parsed document satisfies
  `isinstance(x, PolicyFilterParameters)` and can be handed straight to
  `PolicyFilter` by anyone bypassing the builder, producing an operator that
  admits nothing while looking fully configured. A separate, deliberately
  incomplete contract makes the incompleteness structural
- Migration: `parse_artifact(document, OPERATOR_PARAMETERS_POLICY)`, and the
  document states **no** `permitted_policy_bundle_versions` key — the contract is
  closed-world, so including one is a parse failure. An adopter who expected to
  pass the parsed artifact straight to `PolicyFilter` needs
  `build_operator_registry` instead: it is what constructs the real
  `PolicyFilterParameters`, with the version set taken from the adopter-passed
  pins. The deployment still names which bundles it admits; the SDK only resolves
  the pin

*Recorded by `15-08-operator-parameters.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.OPERATOR_PARAMETERS_TRAVERSAL

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.OPERATOR_PARAMETERS_TRAVERSAL
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no class-id addressed traversal parameters, so a D-05 manifest
  could not name them
- After: `ArtifactClass(class_id="ccf.operator-parameters.traversal", contract=TraversalNeighborhoodParametersArtifact)`,
  registered in `ccf.deployment.classes.SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`
- Why: a class the SDK ships that a manifest cannot address is a class no adopter
  can register; the constant *is* the registration mechanism
- Migration: name `ccf.operator-parameters.traversal` in the manifest entry

*Recorded by `15-08-operator-parameters.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.OPERATOR_PARAMETERS_SIMILARITY

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.OPERATOR_PARAMETERS_SIMILARITY
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `ArtifactClass(class_id="ccf.operator-parameters.similarity", contract=SimilaritySelectParametersArtifact)`,
  registered in `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`
- Why: same as its siblings — manifest addressability for the fourth category

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.SignalBatch

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.SignalBatch
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — every example hand-built envelopes in a comprehension,
  translating three keys per event (`source_system_id` into a nested
  `SourceIdentity`, `payload` into `raw_payload`) and reading `tenant_id` out of
  a different document entirely
- After: a `VersionedArtifact` declaring `tenant_id: str` and
  `envelopes: tuple[SignalEnvelope, ...]` with `min_length=1`, plus a
  `model_validator` refusing a batch whose envelopes name another tenant
- Why: the translation is **removed** rather than relocated. A translation moved
  into `/src` is the same defect with a better address, and a second field
  vocabulary for a shape the SDK already owns is exactly what drifts. The batch
  carries `tenant_id` because every event in a batch belongs to one tenant and
  that value currently lives in the ontology document, which D-03 makes
  closed-world over resolution rules only — carrying it here keeps it authored,
  once, in the artifact that needs it rather than in a loader argument no
  artifact records. Disagreement is refused, never repaired: stamping the batch's
  value onto an envelope that said otherwise would put a value nobody authored
  into the fabric's observation record (T-15-09-01)
- Migration: replace the envelope comprehension with
  `parse_artifact(document, SIGNAL_BATCH)` and reshape the event document per the
  `ccf.signal-batch` entry below. The parsed `envelopes` are real
  `SignalEnvelope` instances and need no unwrapping

*Recorded by `15-09-signal-perspective-productclass.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.SIGNAL_BATCH

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.SIGNAL_BATCH
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no class-id addressed an event batch, so a D-05 manifest could
  not name one
- After: `ArtifactClass(class_id="ccf.signal-batch", contract=SignalBatch)`,
  registered in `ccf.deployment.classes.SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`
- Why: **this is a `role: load` class under D-IMPL-80** and the asymmetry is the
  point. It is parsed and validated through the same `parse_artifact` seam as
  every other class and `load_and_boot` returns it on the boot result, but it is
  never passed to `register_typed` and never appears in the pin set. Registering
  a batch would content-hash raw event data into Control Plane state, and by
  D-06's own rationale the registered artifact set is dimension 2 of the
  five-dimension Decision Replay record — re-authoring an events file would then
  change "what was in force" exactly as a policy-bundle bump does, which is not
  what an event stream is (T-15-09-05)
- Migration: name `ccf.signal-batch` in the manifest entry with `role: load`. A
  manifest entry naming no `role` is refused; one naming `role: register` for
  this class puts an events file into replay dimension 2, which is the mistake
  D-IMPL-80 exists to make impossible to reach by accident

*Recorded by `15-09-signal-perspective-productclass.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.PerspectiveArtifact

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.PerspectiveArtifact
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — `Perspective(principal_id=..., role=...)` built by hand at ten
  sites from two keys read out of a control-plane document
- After: a contract subclassing `ccf.projection.plan.Perspective` verbatim plus
  the `semver` lane
- Why: the same shape plan 15-08 used for the operator-parameters classes. A
  parsed artifact **is** a `Perspective`, so there is no second vocabulary to
  drift and no adapter step between reading the document and conditioning the
  projection. `principal_id` and `role` keep the meanings §7.5 gives them
  (INV-CH7-43) and land verbatim in replay dimension 4
- Migration: `parse_artifact(document, PERSPECTIVE)`; the result passes straight
  into projection invocation with no unwrapping

*Recorded by `15-09-signal-perspective-productclass.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.PERSPECTIVE

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.PERSPECTIVE
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `ArtifactClass(class_id="ccf.perspective", contract=PerspectiveArtifact)`,
  registered in `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`
- Why: manifest addressability — a class the SDK ships that a manifest cannot
  address is a class no adopter can register

*Recorded by `15-09-signal-perspective-productclass.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ProductClassDeclaration

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ProductClassDeclaration
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — `register_product_class(...)` was called with six keyword
  arguments assembled by hand from a control-plane document at ten sites
- After: a `VersionedArtifact` declaring `schema_name`, `schema_version`,
  `ttl_anchor`, `assembly_mode`, `max_ttl_key`, and
  `required_operator_keys: tuple[str, ...]` with `min_length=1`. It declares
  **no** `max_ttl_version`
- Why: two deliberate omissions and one deliberate addition.
  **`max_ttl_version` is absent** because that pin is minted by *registering* the
  `MaxTTL` value and is a content hash of what was registered — the same shape
  D-IMPL-76 ruled on for the policy bundle's admitted-version set. An artifact
  declaring it would be authoring a hash of something it does not contain, and it
  would collapse Gate 3: the TTL ceiling stays an authored artifact value, the pin
  is derived from registering it, and a product's `expires_at` remains a
  separately authored number — Gate 3 checks one against the other, which is only
  a check while the two come from independent places (T-15-09-02).
  **`required_operator_keys` is present** because it is what a projection's
  `required_operators()` returns, and giving it an authored source is what keeps
  the projection's declaration and the engine's resolution two independent
  mechanisms. Gate 2 compares them for strict equality (INV-CH7-47); a projection
  returning the operator registry's own keys makes that comparison a value against
  itself and the gate silently stops being a gate (T-15-09-06). Every example in
  the tree today passes `operators.keys()` or `self._registry().keys()`, which is
  exactly that shortcut; plan 15-21 adds a standing AST guard against it. It lives
  on the product class rather than on a projection artifact because the product
  class is already the artifact that declares what the product *is*, and the
  operator set an interpretation requires is a property of the product, not of the
  code computing it
- Migration: `parse_artifact(document, PRODUCT_CLASS)`, and the document states
  **no** `max_ttl_version` — the contract is closed-world, so including one is a
  parse failure. Keep the `register_product_class` call in adopter code: this plan
  ships no helper that registers on the adopter's behalf, because the manifest
  entry point (15-10) owns registration and a second registration path would make
  "what was in force" answerable from two places. The document must also name at
  least one `required_operator_keys` entry (see below)

*Recorded by `15-09-signal-perspective-productclass.md`.*

- Public surface: true

- Why: manifest addressability, same as its siblings

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ManifestEntry

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ManifestEntry
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — a deployment's artifact set existed only as a sequence of
  hand-written `register`/`register_typed` calls in example code, so "what is in
  force here" was answerable only by reading a script
- After: a contract declaring `path`, `key`, `class_id`, and
  `role: Literal["register", "load"]`, with a `field_validator` refusing any
  path containing `*`, `?`, or `[`
- Why: **`role` is D-IMPL-80 (RESOLVED 2026-08-04, Dave) and it is required with
  no default.** `register` puts the artifact into the Control Plane registry and
  into the pin set; `load` parses and validates it through the same seam and
  hands it back to the caller, but never registers and never pins it. The
  distinction exists because registering an artifact content-hashes it into
  Control Plane state, and by D-06's own rationale the registered artifact set
  is dimension 2 of the five-dimension Decision Replay record (whitepaper
  §7.10.1) — so registering a signal batch would make re-authoring an events
  file change "what was in force" exactly as a policy-bundle bump does. Events
  are what the fabric *observed*; the Control Plane is what it was *permitted to
  do*. The field has **no default** because either default would have the SDK
  deciding that boundary on the adopter's behalf, which is the objection
  `boot()` already raises against every other default (D-IMPL-38). Globs are
  refused for the same replay reason: a pattern would make what was in force a
  function of directory contents at boot time, recorded by no artifact
  (T-15-10-01)
- Migration: enumerate every artifact, one entry each, and state `role` on every
  one — an entry omitting it is a parse failure. Name `ccf.signal-batch` with
  `role: load`; `role: register` on that class is refused outright

*Recorded by `15-10-manifest.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DeploymentManifest

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DeploymentManifest
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: a `VersionedArtifact` declaring `artifacts: tuple[ManifestEntry, ...]`
  with `min_length=1`, `backend: Literal["sqlite", "postgres"]`,
  `resolution_spec_key`, `policy_bundle_key`, and an optional `tenant_id`, plus
  two `model_validator`s — one refusing duplicate keys, one refusing a manifest
  whose two boot pins do not name `role: register` entries
- Why: the manifest is itself a content-hashable artifact, so "what did this
  deployment put in force" is one versioned answer rather than a directory
  listing. **The backend is a kind only (D-08)** — there is no DSN field and no
  database-path field, because a connection string is an environment value that
  `boot()` already documents as never persisted, and a versioned content-hashed
  artifact is exactly where it must not land (T-15-10-05). **Duplicate keys are
  refused (C-9)** because two entries sharing a key make `version_for(key)`
  ambiguous and the boot result's `artifacts[key]` order-dependent, and both
  feed replay; duplicate *paths* under distinct keys are permitted deliberately,
  since one document may legitimately be in force under two names. **`tenant_id`
  is optional (Q3)** because D-IMPL-05 makes tenancy a first-class runtime axis
  and a manifest-pinned tenant would stop one manifest serving a multi-tenant
  deployment — exactly one of the manifest and the caller must supply it, and
  both-absent and a conflicting pair are each refused rather than resolved by
  precedence
- Migration: author one `manifest.yaml` per deployment. The document root
  carries the artifact's own `semver` lane, as every other artifact class does;
  entry paths resolve relative to the manifest file's own directory, never the
  process's working directory

*Recorded by `15-10-manifest.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.manifest.load_and_boot

- Surface: ccf.deployment.manifest.load_and_boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — every deployment hand-wrote its registration prologue and
  then called `boot()` with pins it had collected itself
- After: `load_and_boot(manifest_path, *, registry, database_path=None,
  postgres_dsn=None, tenant_id=None) -> BootedFabric`
- Why: four properties, each of which the hand-written prologue could lose
  silently.
  **Preflight before registration.** The tenant resolution, the D-IMPL-80 role
  check, and the declared-backend-versus-supplied-argument check all run before
  any artifact document is read. A manifest declaring `postgres` with no DSN
  *parses* perfectly well, so a backend check placed beside the `boot` call
  would register every artifact and fail afterwards — leaving Control Plane
  state from a fabric that never booted, which is a replay-integrity problem
  rather than an untidiness (T-15-10-02; raised by cross-AI review against the
  plan's step ordering and verified real).
  **Two-phase parse-then-register.** Every document is parsed before any is
  registered, so a malformed document at entry seven leaves the registry
  untouched instead of half-populated (T-15-10-08). A failure *inside* the
  registration loop can still leave earlier entries registered; that is accepted
  and documented rather than rolled back, because registration is
  content-addressed and audited, so a corrected re-run registers identical
  content to identical versions and the audit log shows both attempts — a
  Control Plane that silently un-registers is worse than one that records a
  retry.
  **No `resolve_latest`, ever.** Its absence from the module is asserted; that
  is the "latest" `boot()` refuses (T-15-10-04).
  **No registry constructed here.** The Control Plane is a caller argument, so
  the adopter keeps one audit sequence; a registry built behind the adopter's
  back would split it in two (whitepaper §7.8.2)
- Migration: replace the registration prologue and the `boot()` call with one
  `load_and_boot`. Supply exactly the connection argument the manifest's
  declared backend needs — supplying the other one, or neither, is refused.
  The returned handle's disposal contract is unchanged: `booted.handle.close()`,
  or `with booted.handle:`. One call is easy to put in a loop over manifests or
  tenants, and a loop that never closes leaks a connection per iteration and
  will eventually be refused a boot by the single-writer check

*Recorded by `15-10-manifest.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.manifest.BootedFabric

- Surface: ccf.deployment.manifest.BootedFabric
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — `boot()` returned a bare `FabricHandle` and the caller kept
  its pins in local variables
- After: a frozen dataclass with exactly four attributes: `handle: FabricHandle`,
  `pins: ResolvedPins`, `tenant_id: str`, and
  `artifacts: Mapping[str, CCFContract]`
- Why: a dataclass rather than a `CCFContract` because it carries a live handle,
  which has no canonical serialization and never belongs in a content hash —
  the reasoning `ArtifactClass` already records. **`artifacts` carries both
  roles; `pins` carries `register` only.** `artifacts` is D-IMPL-80's return
  path: the ruling requires `role: load` entries to come back to the caller
  rather than being registered, and returning the register-role ones alongside
  them costs nothing and closes a second gap — without it, an adopter needing
  the parsed operator-parameters artifacts would re-read them off disk or
  `isinstance`-filter them out of the registry, and that filtering idiom copied
  across nine examples is a new duplication class of exactly the sort this phase
  exists to remove. `role` governs registration and pinning, **never
  visibility**: a `role: load` artifact appearing in `artifacts` is still absent
  from `pins`, from the registry, and from replay dimension 2
- Migration: `booted.handle` is the old return value; `booted.pins` replaces the
  local version variables; `booted.artifacts[key]` replaces re-reading a
  document. Keep the MAX_TTL and product-class registrations in adopter code —
  the TTL pin must stay an independently authored registration or Gate 3
  compares a ceiling against itself

*Recorded by `15-10-manifest.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.pins.ResolvedPins

- Surface: ccf.deployment.pins.ResolvedPins
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — pins lived as loose local variables named `spec_version`,
  `policy_version`, and so on
- After: a contract carrying `entries: tuple[ArtifactPin, ...]` and **exactly
  one** public method, `version_for(key) -> str`
- Why: the constraint is the design. A convenience like
  `pins.policy_bindings()` would shorten the adopter's
  `build_replay_record(policy_bindings=...)` line while moving the *selection*
  of which bindings are in force out of the deployment's code and into the SDK —
  a governance-boundary move wearing an ergonomics costume, and dimension 2 of
  the replay record then assembled by a rule nobody wrote down. `version_for` is
  **lookup**, not selection: the caller already knows the key it wants. The
  record carries `role: register` entries only (D-IMPL-80), so `version_for`
  raising for a `role: load` key is correct behavior rather than a lookup bug —
  a load-role artifact has no version because nothing registered it. A test pins
  the public method set exactly, so a later convenience fails CI rather than
  landing quietly
- Migration: replace the loose version variables with
  `booted.pins.version_for(key)`, naming the manifest key

*Recorded by `15-10-manifest.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.classes.SDK_ARTIFACT_ROLES

- Surface: ccf.deployment.classes.SDK_ARTIFACT_ROLES
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — D-IMPL-80 was defended by docstrings on `SignalBatch` and
  `SIGNAL_BATCH` and a comment in `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`, all of which a
  manifest author can simply not read
- After: `dict[str, ArtifactRole]` mapping a class id to the role the SDK
  **fixes** for it. Exactly one entry today: `ccf.signal-batch -> "load"`
- Why: 15-09 recorded that nothing yet *prevented* an adopter writing
  `role: register` against `ccf.signal-batch`, and that no CI failure would
  fire — leaving the ruling enforced by prose. This is the machine-checkable
  half: the required `role` field stops the choice being made silently, and
  this mapping stops it being made wrongly for the one class where the SDK
  knows the answer. It lives beside the class registry because the fact is a
  property of the *class*, not of any manifest. **Every class absent from the
  mapping is the adopter's call, and that is the default** — the SDK fixes a
  role only where it can say why (T-15-10-07)
- Migration: none, unless a manifest declared `role: register` against
  `ccf.signal-batch`, which is now refused before anything is registered

  audit plane reads back

#### ccf.deployment.operators.build_operator_registry

- Surface: ccf.deployment.operators.build_operator_registry
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — every deployment hand-wrote a ~64-line block constructing four
  operators, converting the policy artifact's fields into a
  `PolicyFilterParameters`, and building the similarity corpus with an inline
  dict comprehension over a live view
- After: `build_operator_registry(*, parameters, view, pins, policy_bundle_key,
  similarity_index_version=None) -> OperatorRegistry`
- Why: four properties, each of which the hand-written block could lose silently.
  **The view is in the signature.** This could not be a pure artifact read:
  `SimilaritySelect` ranks against an injected index built from a corpus read out
  of the live graph at a named temporal boundary, after boot and after ingest.
  Lazy binding — handing the operator a callable that built the index at first
  invocation — was considered and rejected: it would build the index inside the
  projection engine's execution, in the algorithmic path, over a corpus
  determined by *when the engine happened to invoke the operator* rather than by
  a boundary the deployment named. INV-CH7-27 fixes the similarity determinism
  contract as `(corpus_version, embedding_model_version, query, threshold,
  policy_regime) -> same result`, and a corpus that is a function of invocation
  timing is not one that contract can name. `view` is therefore keyword-only with
  no default, so the ordering constraint is structural rather than documented,
  and a test asserts `Parameter.empty` rather than merely asserting that omitting
  it raises (T-15-11-03).
  **The admitted policy-bundle version arrives from the adopter.** Per D-IMPL-76
  the pin set is passed *in*, never discovered: there is no `resolve_latest` in
  the module, and a builder that asked the Control Plane what to admit would be
  the fabric deciding its own admission set. The `PolicyFilterParameters` is
  constructed explicitly rather than by `model_copy(update=...)` — the artifact
  is not a `PolicyFilterParameters`, so there is nothing to copy from, and the
  explicit construction is the single visible place where the admitted-bundle set
  is assembled (T-15-11-01).
  **No caller-declared category.** Registration goes through `register(operator)`
  with no `category=` keyword; the registry reads category and parameter schema
  off the operator. A builder that re-introduced a caller-declared category would
  let the planner order an operator into a position it does not belong in — the
  Policy-after-Similarity failure INV-CH7-26 forbids. An AST test asserts zero
  `category=` keywords across every `register` call in the module, because a
  behavioral test would pass if a future edit declared each operator's *own*
  category explicitly, which reads harmlessly and reopens the failure anyway
  (T-15-11-02).
  **Which operators exist stays per-deployment policy.** Expressed by which
  artifacts `parameters` contains — `examples/02-skilling` registers three, not
  four — so the module holds no fixed set of four keys, no operator key literal
  and no entity class literal (CINV-01). `DuplicateOperatorKey` stays fatal and
  uncaught (T-15-11-04)
- Migration: replace the inline block with the call. Assemble `parameters` by
  naming your own manifest keys against `booted.artifacts` — no `isinstance`
  filtering, which is what the `OperatorParametersArtifact` marker base from
  15-08 exists to make unnecessary. Take the view *at the boundary the decision
  is being made against*, before calling. Pass `booted.pins` and the manifest's
  `policy_bundle_key`. Supply `similarity_index_version` exactly when
  `parameters` carries a similarity artifact: it is required then and refused
  otherwise. **The builder does not mint one** — a version derived from the
  parameters artifact's own pin would be identical across two different
  boundaries, which is precisely the corpus identity INV-CH7-27 needs to
  distinguish, and `TokenOverlapIndex` already documents `index_version` as a
  value the deployment assigns

*Recorded by `15-11-operator-builder.md`.*

- Change type: added

#### ccf.deployment.operators.SimilarityCorpusAttributeError

- Surface: ccf.deployment.operators.SimilarityCorpusAttributeError
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the inline corpus comprehension raised a bare `KeyError` from
  a dict subscript, with no message naming which artifact and which entity
  disagreed, and nine sites were free to "fix" it nine different ways
- After: a `ValueError` raised at registry-build time when an entity of a class
  the corpus map *names* carries no such attribute, or carries a non-string value
  under it
- Why: **this is the corpus-extraction ruling, and it is stated because nine
  deployments will follow it.** Two things can go wrong reading a corpus through
  `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class`, and they get opposite answers.
  *An entity class absent from the map contributes no corpus entry* — silent, and
  correct. The map is the deployment's statement of which classes are rankable, so
  a class it does not name is a class the deployment chose to leave out, exactly
  as `SimilaritySelectParametersArtifact` already records. There is nothing to
  repair and nothing to report.
  *An entity of a named class carrying no such attribute is refused.* The
  declaration and the graph contradict each other, and every repair is worse than
  the refusal: skipping the entity drops it from the corpus, `TokenOverlapIndex.rank`
  then silently passes over it, and the similarity operator excludes it from the
  view — a governed omission produced by no rule anyone wrote down. Substituting
  an empty string is the same omission with a scoring story attached. **Refuse,
  never repair.** A non-string corpus value is refused by the same rule: coercing
  it would move the failure into ranking, at invocation time, far from the
  artifact that declared it. Both halves are asserted, not left to the docstring
- Migration: none for a correct deployment. If a build now raises this, the
  similarity artifact's `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class` names an attribute
  the resolution spec does not carry onto that entity class — fix whichever of
  the two documents is wrong. Removing the class from the map is the supported
  way to say "this class is not rankable"

*Recorded by `15-11-operator-builder.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.cli.commands.schema.register

- Surface: ccf_runtime.cli.commands.schema.register
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no CLI path to an artifact class's JSON Schema
- After: the ninth noun, `ccf schema export [--class-id ID]`, returning
  `RequestSpec`s against `/schema` exactly like the other eight
- Why: D-IMPL-79. `ccf schema export` (D-04) is in-process JSON Schema
  generation with no fabric handle behind it, so the obvious implementation
  is a local path in the CLI — which would be `main()`'s first
  non-`RequestSpec` branch and would weaken the documented posture from
  "every command travels the same routes any client uses" to "every command
  except schema export". The first exception is the one that makes the
  second easy, so the route was added instead and the noun stayed uniform.
  D-IMPL-22's noun enumeration is amended to nine as the mechanical
  consequence; `tests/http_api/test_routes.py` reads that text to hold the
  mounted prefixes to it.
- Migration: none — a new noun; nothing existing moved

*Recorded by `15-12-schema-export.md`.*

#### ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.schema.build_router

- Surface: ccf_runtime.http_api.routes.schema.build_router
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the SDK's artifact-class schemas were not reachable over
  the wire
- After: two read-only endpoints, `GET /schema` (the class ids the SDK owns
  the schema of) and `GET /schema/{class_id}` (that class's JSON Schema),
  emitted from the live models with an unowned id returning a typed 404
- Why: the route D-IMPL-79 rules the CLI noun onto. It generates from
  `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` rather than reading `docs/schemas/` off disk, on
  the same relation `create_app().openapi()` has to the committed
  `openapi.json` — a route serving the committed files would make the
  freshness gate circular, agreeing with itself and with nothing else. The
  route holds no fabric handle: artifact classes are SDK mechanism, not
  deployment state. Note the path prefix is singular `/schema`, matching the
  CLI noun and the other eight prefixes, rather than the plural the plan
  text used.
- Migration: none — a new read-only surface

*Recorded by `15-12-schema-export.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ProjectionRequirements

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ProjectionRequirements
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — a projection plan's `required_operators()` had exactly one
  authored source available to it, `ProductClassDeclaration.required_operator_keys`,
  which is also the value that fills `lineage.operators_invoked`
- After: a `VersionedArtifact` declaring `plan_id: str` and
  `required_operator_keys: tuple[str, ...]` with `min_length=1`
- Why: with one document, the two sides of Gate 2 (lineage strict equality,
  INV-CH7-47) are not merely equal in practice — they are identical by construction.
  `execution.operators_invoked` is `frozenset(declared.required_operator_keys)`
  through five pure transforms with no branch: the declaration feeds `plan.required`;
  `plan.required_operators()` returns it; `build_operator_plan` resolves each key
  self-keyed so `resolve(k).key == k`; the engine writes `outputs[operator.key]`; and
  it returns `operators_invoked=frozenset(outputs)`. Nothing a deployment could author
  would make the two sides diverge, which is why the remedy first recorded for CR-2
  was withdrawn as circular. Under design E2 the projection reads **this** artifact
  and the product class keeps the claim, so the two sets are separately authored and a
  one-key edit to either document is reachable drift (D-IMPL-86; T-15.1-01-02). An
  artifact rather than operator keys in a projection class body, because under the
  rejected design the compliant state (an author typed the keys) and the violating
  state (an author transcribed them from the product-class document) are textually
  identical and no AST gate can distinguish them — E2 satisfies the No-Hardcoding Rule
  (CLAUDE.md §6.4) by construction rather than by argument
- Migration: nothing breaks today; this plan adds the contract and no caller. From
  plan 15.1-05 onward a deployment authors a second document of class
  `ccf.projection-requirements` naming its plan and that plan's operator keys, and
  builds its `ProjectionPlan` from it rather than from the product-class declaration.
  The binder added in plan 15.1-04 — placed between plan construction and
  `ProjectionEngine.execute`, per D-IMPL-86's correction of 2026-08-05 — refuses when
  the two documents disagree

*Recorded by `15.1-01-projection-requirements-and-protocol.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.PROJECTION_REQUIREMENTS

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.PROJECTION_REQUIREMENTS
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no class id addressed a projection's own operator declaration, so
  a D-05 manifest could not name one
- After: `ArtifactClass(class_id="ccf.projection-requirements", contract=ProjectionRequirements)`,
  registered in `ccf.deployment.classes.SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`
- Why: manifest addressability, on the same rule as every sibling — a class the SDK
  ships that a manifest cannot address is a class no adopter can register. It matters
  more here than usual: the binder's comparison has two authored inputs and this
  document is one of them, so a deployment that could not register it could not author
  half of the check
- Migration: name `ccf.projection-requirements` in the manifest entry, with
  `role: register`

*Recorded by `15.1-01-projection-requirements-and-protocol.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.RegistrationMode

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.RegistrationMode
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the two registration paths were reachable as two method names on the
  Control Plane and by nothing a document could state
- After: `RegistrationMode = Literal["typed", "versioned"]`, declared beside
  `ArtifactRole` and exported from `ccf.controlplane.artifacts.__all__`
- Why: Phase 15.1's criterion 2 is that an artifact author does not have to know which
  of two registration paths their class belongs to. That is only true if the manifest
  *states* the mode, and stating it needs a name for the thing being stated. The two
  values are the two paths and no more: `typed` is `register_typed` — class binding,
  contract validation, content hash, audit; `versioned` is `register_versioned`, which
  does all of that plus the D-05 bump check against the author's declared
  `artifact_version`
- Migration: none on its own. It is the type of the new `ManifestEntry.registration` and
  `ArtifactPin.registration` fields below; an adopter reads it, and writes one of its
  two string values in YAML

*Recorded by `15.1-02-registration-mode.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.classes.SDK_VERSIONED_ARTIFACT_CLASSES

- Surface: ccf.deployment.classes.SDK_VERSIONED_ARTIFACT_CLASSES
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the six SDK-owned classes whose contracts declare a string
  `artifact_version` (`ccf.agent-definition`, `ccf.prompt-artifact`,
  `ccf.task-definition`, `ccf.workflow-definition`, `ccf.skill-catalog-entry`,
  `ccf.skill-grant`) were in no lookup mapping at all, so a manifest naming one
  resolved the open `DeclaredArtifact` envelope
- After: `dict[str, ArtifactClass]` holding exactly those six, disjoint from
  `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` by construction, consulted by `resolve_artifact_class` after it
  and before the envelope
- Why: `registration: versioned` (15.1-02) gave a manifest entry a way to *ask* for the
  D-05 bump check, and nothing gave `resolve_artifact_class` a way to hand
  `register_versioned` an artifact that actually carries an `artifact_version`. Without
  this mapping the `versioned` path could only ever reach the envelope, and
  `register_versioned` raises `TypeError` on an envelope. Kept separate from
  `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` on purpose — that mapping is a runtime contract three surfaces
  read, and "typed-registerable" and "versioned-registerable" are genuinely different
  questions. `tests/deployment/test_class_lookup.py` computes the author-versioned set by
  walking the SDK's exported `ArtifactClass` constants and asserts membership in both
  directions, so the mapping cannot fall behind and cannot acquire a stowaway
- Migration: none required. A deployment that wants the bump check on one of the six may
  now name it in the manifest with `registration: versioned` instead of writing the
  `registry.register_versioned(...)` call by hand; existing hand-written calls are
  untouched and remain correct

*Recorded by `15.1-03-namespace-reservation.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.SDK_VERSIONED_ARTIFACT_CLASSES

- Surface: ccf.deployment.SDK_VERSIONED_ARTIFACT_CLASSES
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: the package-level re-export of
  `ccf.deployment.classes.SDK_VERSIONED_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`, added to
  `ccf.deployment.__all__` beside `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` and `SDK_ARTIFACT_ROLES`
- Why: the package `__all__` is the import path adopters and tests actually use —
  `from ccf.deployment import SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` is the existing idiom, and a new
  mapping reachable only at the submodule would be a second, inconsistent spelling
- Migration: none

*Recorded by `15.1-03-namespace-reservation.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.classes.refuse_registration_the_sdk_fixes

- Surface: ccf.deployment.classes.refuse_registration_the_sdk_fixes
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — referenced by name in 15.1-02's docstrings as forthcoming. A manifest
  could declare either registration mode for any class, and the only feedback was a
  `TypeError` raised from inside `register_versioned` *after* every document had been
  parsed, or silence in the `typed` direction
- After: `refuse_registration_the_sdk_fixes(class_id: str, registration: RegistrationMode)
  -> None`, shaped exactly like `refuse_role_the_sdk_fixes`. Raises `ValueError` naming
  D-IMPL-85 in two cases and only two: an author-versioned class declared `typed` (the
  D-05 bump check would not run) and a typed-only SDK class declared `versioned` (the
  bump check would have nothing to verify). Called from `load_and_boot`'s preflight loop,
  **before any document is read**
- Why: D-IMPL-85 made `registration` a required no-default field so the choice is never
  made *silently*; this is the half that stops it being made *wrongly* for the classes
  where the answer is a property of the contract rather than of the deployment — the same
  split `role` and `refuse_role_the_sdk_fixes` already carry (D-IMPL-80). A class in
  neither mapping is not checked at all, and that is the default: the SDK fixes a
  registration path only where it can say why. Refused, never corrected — silently
  rewriting the entry would leave the manifest saying one thing and the deployment doing
  another, and the manifest is the document the audit plane reads back
- Migration: a manifest entry naming one of the six author-versioned classes must declare
  `registration: versioned`, and an entry naming any class in `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` must
  declare `registration: typed`. No manifest in this repository was affected — no example
  or fixture manifest named a `ccf.`-prefixed class outside `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`. The
  refusal fires in preflight, so a wrong declaration costs a startup error rather than
  partial Control Plane state

*Recorded by `15.1-03-namespace-reservation.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.refuse_registration_the_sdk_fixes

- Surface: ccf.deployment.refuse_registration_the_sdk_fixes
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: the package-level re-export of
  `ccf.deployment.classes.refuse_registration_the_sdk_fixes`, added to
  `ccf.deployment.__all__` beside `refuse_role_the_sdk_fixes`
- Why: same reason as the mapping above — `refuse_role_the_sdk_fixes` is already exported
  at the package level, and its sibling refusal being reachable only at the submodule
  would be a gratuitous asymmetry in the surface adopters read
- Migration: none

*Recorded by `15.1-03-namespace-reservation.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.binder.refuse_operator_set_divergence

- Surface: ccf.deployment.binder.refuse_operator_set_divergence
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent, and there was nothing for it to compare. The operator set a deployment
  declared and the set its projection executed traced to **one** artifact through five pure
  transforms, so nothing a deployment could author made them diverge — the divergence this
  function refuses was not reachable by editing a document, only by writing a bug
- After: `refuse_operator_set_divergence(*, declared: ProductClassDeclaration, plan:
  ProjectionPlan) -> None`. Raises `ValueError` naming the plan id, both operator sets, their
  symmetric difference and D-IMPL-86 when
  `frozenset(declared.required_operator_keys) != frozenset(plan.required_operators())`. Two
  separately authored Control Plane artifacts, bound by a refusal placed **between plan
  construction and `ProjectionEngine.execute`**. Refuse-never-repair: the binder does not
  choose which document is right, because only the deployment's author knows. It returns
  `None` and there is deliberately no variant that returns the set — a binder that handed the
  declared set back would put the SDK in the position of supplying one of Gate 2's two
  arguments, which is the D-15A line `cinv02-deployment-gates` holds
- Why: D-IMPL-86 design E2 element (3), as corrected 2026-08-05. **Drift must be authorable**
  — Dave's ruling of record. Without this comparison the two documents are kept in sync by
  hand with no mechanism binding them, which was the standing objection to introducing a
  second artifact at all; the refusal is that mechanism. The placement is the correction's,
  not element (3)'s: `load_and_boot` takes no `plan` parameter and has no `ProjectionPlan` in
  scope at any preflight, so a comparison naming `plan.required_operators()` cannot run at
  boot
- Migration: a deployment adopting the `ccf.projection-requirements` artifact builds its plan
  from that document and calls `refuse_operator_set_divergence(declared=…, plan=…)` between
  constructing the plan and calling `ProjectionEngine.execute`. **The SDK does not enforce
  that call outside its own `examples/` tree.** An adopter who omits it gets no error, no
  warning and no degraded output — the function returns `None`, so nothing observable at
  runtime proves it was ever made, and the AST coverage check that enforces the call
  (`tests/products/test_gate_two_call_shape.py`, plan 15.1-08) is scoped to `examples/` only.
  This residual is named by `D-IMPL-86 (correction, 2026-08-05)`, is carried to Phase 16, and
  is surfaced at Phase 15.1's gate for Dave to rule on. Nothing existing breaks: no current
  deployment authored a projection-requirements document, so no current deployment can
  diverge

*Recorded by `15.1-04-operator-set-binder.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.refuse_operator_set_divergence

- Surface: ccf.deployment.refuse_operator_set_divergence
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: the package-level re-export of
  `ccf.deployment.binder.refuse_operator_set_divergence`, added to `ccf.deployment.__all__`
  beside `refuse_registration_the_sdk_fixes` and `refuse_role_the_sdk_fixes`
- Why: the package `__all__` is the import path adopters and examples actually use, and the
  package's two other refusals are already exported there; a third reachable only at the
  submodule would be a gratuitous asymmetry in the surface adopters read
- Migration: none

*Recorded by `15.1-04-operator-set-binder.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.protocols.GraphQuery

- Surface: ccf.graph.protocols.GraphQuery
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the only question the read seam could carry was
  `as_of(tenant_id, at)`, so nothing below an operator could be asked a
  predicate and pushdown had no vocabulary to travel in
- After: a frozen `CCFContract` with required `tenant_id` and `as_of`, the
  three optional predicate families (policy mirroring `PolicyFilterParameters`
  field-for-field, temporal carrying `require_valid_at_boundary`, traverse as
  `TraversalSpec | None`), explicit `seeds`, and
  `allowed_entities: frozenset[str] | None = None` — the D-IMPL-87 survivor
  set, sentinel-defaulted so the common no-Similarity case is untouched.
  Construction refuses a partially-specified policy family (`GQ-02`) and
  `seeds` without a traverse family (`GQ-03`)
- Why: the question must be a value (D-02): `content_hash(q)` is replay's
  per-round-trip pin, so the query has to be frozen, canonically hashable,
  and closed under `extra=forbid`. `allowed_entities` implements the
  D-IMPL-87 resolution's uniform rule — every pushed segment after an
  in-process segment carries the surviving entity set, closing the
  entity-resurrection defect — and it is part of the question's identity, so
  a capped execution and a pushed execution can never share a pin. Per D-12
  the contract carries no version field: the vocabulary is versioned through
  the `docs/GRAPH_QUERY_SEMANTICS.md` semver lane only
- Migration: new surface — none. `as_of()` call sites are unaffected; plan
  15.2-05 derives `as_of()` from the degenerate query (D-07) with no caller
  change

*Recorded by `15.2-04-graph-query-contracts.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.protocols.TraversalSpec

- Surface: ccf.graph.protocols.TraversalSpec
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — traversal bounds existed only as the operator-tier
  `TraversalNeighborhoodParameters`, which the graph tier cannot import
  (`ccf.operators` imports `ccf.graph`, not the reverse)
- After: a frozen `CCFContract` mirroring `TraversalNeighborhoodParameters`
  field-for-field — `seed_entity_classes: frozenset[str]`,
  `relationship_types: frozenset[str]`, `depth_limit: int` — all required, no
  defaults (D-IMPL-38 discipline)
- Why: the traverse family of `GQ-02` needs a graph-tier rendering so a
  pushed traversal and an in-process traversal are parameterized by the same
  three facts; a mirror rather than a reuse keeps the dependency pointing one
  way
- Migration: new surface — none

*Recorded by `15.2-04-graph-query-contracts.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.protocols.QueryFragment

- Surface: ccf.graph.protocols.QueryFragment
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — an operator had no typed way to contribute its predicate
  family to a segment's query
- After: a frozen `CCFContract` carrying at most one of the three predicate
  families (refused at construction otherwise, per `GQ-06`) and structurally
  nothing else: no `seeds` field, no `allowed_entities` field
- Why: the union rule (`GQ-06`) needs a typed unit — fragments union
  field-wise into one `GraphQuery`, at most one fragment per family, and a
  same-family conflict is a broken plan refused at compilation. The absent
  `allowed_entities` field is the T-15.2-26 mitigation by construction: the
  survivor set is engine-authored segment-boundary data, so no operator's
  declared intent can widen or replace the constraint
- Migration: new surface — none. Operators gain `read_intent()` returning
  this type in a later plan; nothing existing produces or consumes fragments
  yet

*Recorded by `15.2-04-graph-query-contracts.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.protocols.SourceAttestation

- Surface: ccf.graph.protocols.SourceAttestation
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — a graph-state source had no typed way to state how its
  answers acquired policy binding and lineage, so admitting a lower-trust
  source class would have forced a schema refactor
- After: a frozen `CCFContract` (SDK-defined, CLAUDE.md §11) with
  `policy_binding: Literal["assembly-stamped", "admission-verified", "none"]`,
  `lineage: Literal["graph-supplied", "product-carried", "none"]`, and
  `peer: PeerRef | None` — the D-09 modes verbatim
- Why: D-09 rules the three trust tiers to be three well-known values of one
  schema, and tier 3 (`none`/`none`/`None`) must be expressible today — the
  no-refactor commitment met by construction and pinned by test. Temporal
  correctness is deliberately not a dimension: the as-of cut is a conformance
  obligation every answerer owes (D-04), so there is no mode to report.
  Canonically serializable, so the attestation in force pins into replay with
  zero new mechanism
- Migration: new surface — none. `GraphReader` gains an `attestation`
  property in plan 15.2-05; per-dimension product-class minima and the engine
  refusal arrive in later plans

*Recorded by `15.2-04-graph-query-contracts.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.fallback

- Surface: ccf.graph.fallback.answer
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the query semantics existed as prose
  (`docs/GRAPH_QUERY_SEMANTICS.md`) with no executable reference, and every
  adapter would have grown its own scan-and-filter
- After: `answer(entities, relationships, *, query)` — pure functions over
  materialized records implementing the prose clause-for-clause: the as-of
  survival predicate re-applied over retained temporal fields (`GQ-12` —
  INV-CH7-14 independently enforceable at this tier), the validity window
  (`GQ-09`), policy admissibility (`GQ-08`), the D-IMPL-87
  `allowed_entities` set intersection applied before any family evaluates
  (`GQ-15`, empty set answers the empty view per `GQ-04`), endpoint closure
  on constrained answers (`GQ-16`), and the bounded typed undirected BFS
  with seed inclusion and prune-before-walk hop admissibility (`GQ-10`,
  `GQ-11`, `GQ-13`). No ordering, no freezing — the interface owns both
  (`GQ-22`)
- Why: D-05 artifact 2 — the one BFS every adapter degrades to, audited by
  the property suite (`tests/conformance/test_substrate_properties.py`)
  against INV-CH7-14/-23/-24/-26 so the oracle is answerable to commitments
  it did not author; D-04's always-answer posture is only honest if the
  degrade path exists once, centrally
- Migration: new surface — none. Adapter authors call `answer()` for
  whatever their substrate did not translate natively; they never
  re-implement its semantics

*Recorded by `15.2-05-query-seam-open.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.memory.MemoryGraphStore

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.memory.MemoryGraphStore
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the fabric's cheapest substrate was a SQLite temp file;
  no fabric could boot with zero filesystem or server dependencies
- After: `MemoryGraphStore()` — per-tenant dict tables mirroring the
  relational shape field-for-field, canonical RFC3339 text timestamps, and a
  synthetic per-instance `memory://` `graph_identity` so the single-writer
  boot check keys on instance identity (two writers over one instance are
  the violation; two instances are two fabrics). Offers `reader`,
  `close()`, and context management — the same envelope as the SQLite store
- Why: the Phase 15.2 D-06 ruling — a zero-dependency floor that is
  always-on in `make ci` and doubles as the teaching example of an adapter
  with no query engine
- Migration: new surface — none. `boot(memory=True)` constructs the store;
  direct construction is only for wiring the reader into read-only harnesses

*Recorded by `15.2-06-memory-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.memory.MemoryGraphReader

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.memory.MemoryGraphReader
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: a `GraphReader` conformer with no query engine and no native
  translation, by design: it acquires as-of rows from the dicts (the row
  filter over canonical text — lexicographic is chronological), materializes
  them through the substrate-neutral core
  (`ccf.graph.reader.materialize_view`), answers every query through
  `ccf.graph.fallback.answer` (`allowed_entities` handled there by
  set-intersection — no memory-specific code), and freezes through the
  interface-owned ordering (`GQ-22`). `as_of()` is the D-07 degenerate-query
  derivation; `attestation` reports tier 1
- Why: D-06 names this the teaching example — the shape every adapter's
  degrade path already has (`GQ-29`, D-04), and the proof that
  lineage-at-boundary, supersession-as-of, ordering, and traversal exist
  exactly once in the tree (15.2-RESEARCH.md Pitfall 5)
- Migration: new surface — none. Adapter authors copy this shape for their
  own degrade paths

*Recorded by `15.2-06-memory-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.operators.base.read_intent_of

- Surface: ccf.operators.base.read_intent_of
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — nothing could ask an operator whether its `apply()` has a
  substrate rendering
- After: `read_intent_of(operator) -> QueryFragment | None` — the one
  sanctioned probe for the optional pushability capability; `None` for any
  operator that does not implement it
- Why: D-03 rules that operators declare pushability from `self.parameters`
  alone; centralizing the probe keeps the segment split one function of the
  plan (`GQ-14`), never a caller-by-caller `hasattr` convention
- Migration: new surface — none; custom operators may optionally implement
  `read_intent()`

*Recorded by `15.2-07-read-intent-segmentation.md`.*

#### ccf.operators.base.PushableOperator

- Surface: ccf.operators.base.PushableOperator
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: a `runtime_checkable` Protocol declaring
  `read_intent() -> QueryFragment | None`, separate from `Operator` so
  pre-D-03 operators still satisfy the operator contract unchanged
- Why: the capability is optional by design (D-03); a new required member on
  `Operator` would break every existing third-party operator
- Migration: new surface — none; implement it to make a custom operator
  pushable without any SDK change

*Recorded by `15.2-07-read-intent-segmentation.md`.*

#### ccf.projection.segmentation.segment_plan

- Surface: ccf.projection.segmentation.segment_plan
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no plan-splitting mechanism existed; the engine performed
  a single everything-read
- After: `segment_plan(operators) -> SegmentedPlan` — pure (no reader, no
  clock, no I/O): maximal runs of fragment-declaring operators form pushable
  segments, `None`-intent runs form in-process segments, and every
  alternation opens with a pushable segment (the implicit degenerate
  everything-question when nothing pushable leads, `GQ-05`)
- Why: the D-IMPL-87 resolution — the split is a function of the plan, never
  the substrate (`GQ-14`), so round-trip count and provenance are identical
  on every adapter
- Migration: new surface — none

*Recorded by `15.2-07-read-intent-segmentation.md`.*

- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `compile_segment_query(segment, *, tenant_id, as_of, seeds,

#### ccf.projection.segmentation.FragmentFamilyConflict

- Surface: ccf.projection.segmentation.FragmentFamilyConflict
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `ValueError` raised at plan compilation when two fragments in one
  segment claim the same predicate family (`GQ-06` — refused, never merged)
- Why: a merge rule would silently decide which operator's parameters win,
  and a decided-by-accident parameter set cannot be replayed against an
  intent; an exception because a colliding plan is a broken deployment, not
  an expected outcome
- Migration: new surface — none

*Recorded by `15.2-07-read-intent-segmentation.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.NO_GRAPH_QUERIES

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.NO_GRAPH_QUERIES
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — dimension 5 had no query-pin field and therefore no
  sentinel for its absence
- After: `Final[tuple[str, ...]] = ()` — the positive assertion "this
  decision was recorded before the query seam existed / issued no seam
  reads", in the `NO_WORKFLOW_TRACE` voice
- Why: D-11 pins are recorded from execution; a record that predates the
  seam must assert so rather than leave a field a reader interprets — an
  absent pin sequence and a dropped one would otherwise be the same artifact
- Migration: new surface — none; records lacking the field rebuild to this
  sentinel

*Recorded by `15.2-08-replay-query-pins.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.serve.QueryPinWriter

- Surface: ccf.replay.serve.QueryPinWriter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — nothing persisted an issued query's literal bytes
- After: constructed like `ReplayWriter` (`store` + `artifact_class`, both
  deployment-supplied; examples/tests use `"ccf.graph-query"`);
  `write_query_bytes(tenant_id, queries)` puts each executed `GraphQuery`
  content-addressed, named by its own `content_hash` — the same value the
  record pins, and equal to the store's derived address
- Why: D-11 — an auditor holding a pinned hash resolves the literal question
  from the store without re-running anything; content addressing converges
  duplicate questions on one artifact
- Migration: new surface — none; deployments on the governed path construct
  one beside their `ReplayWriter`

*Recorded by `15.2-08-replay-query-pins.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.serve.QueryPinMismatch

- Surface: ccf.replay.serve.QueryPinMismatch
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `ValueError` raised by `serve()` before anything is persisted when
  the record's pinned sequence and the queries handed to the seam disagree —
  or when queries arrive with no writer to persist them, or a configured
  writer meets a pinned record with no queries
- Why: T-15.2-28 — a record appended beside bytes it does not pin is a pin
  an auditor can never resolve; refusing before any store write keeps that
  unrepresentable on the governed path
- Migration: new surface — none

*Recorded by `15.2-08-replay-query-pins.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.product_source.ProductSource

- Surface: ccf.graph.product_source.ProductSource
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the only tier-2 story was admission-then-L2-ingest; an
  admitted product could enter the graph through L2 but could not serve as
  an ephemeral read source
- After: a `GraphReader` conformer over a mounted admitted product's
  `graph_state` records. No query engine: `ccf.graph.fallback.answer` is
  the whole implementation — its re-applied as-of predicate is the records'
  only temporal enforcement (`GQ-12`) and its set-intersection the only
  `allowed_entities` enforcement (`GQ-15`) — with ordering/freezing through
  the substrate-neutral materialization core (`GQ-22`). A tenant-mismatched
  query answers the empty view, never a mix and never a raise (`GQ-27`).
  `attestation` reports the tier-2 well-known triple: `admission-verified`
  / `product-carried` / the admitting peer (D-09). Views are ephemeral —
  nothing enters L3; the module holds no writer
- Why: the Phase 15.2 D-08 ruling — same seam, admitted at mount; tier 3
  later is another gate producing the same wrapper, zero refactor here
- Migration: new surface — none. Construct via `mount_product_source`; the
  direct constructor is private by convention

*Recorded by `15.2-10-product-source.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.product_source.mount_product_source

- Surface: ccf.graph.product_source.mount_product_source
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `mount_product_source(*, admitted: AdmittedProduct) ->
  ProductSource | ProductMountRefusal` — the one door. Validates the
  `GQ-25` payload convention (top-level `graph_state` with
  `entities`/`relationships` items `model_validate`d against the existing
  `EntityRecord`/`RelationshipRecord` contracts) and refuses records
  claiming a tenant other than the product's own (T-15.2-02). Refusal is a
  returned value, never an exception. No payload-size ceiling of its own:
  the admission gate's Control Plane ceiling runs before an
  `AdmittedProduct` can exist
- Why: D-08/§6.6 — a source that cannot answer correctly is refused while
  wiring, not discovered mid-question (`GQ-25`)
- Migration: new surface — none

*Recorded by `15.2-10-product-source.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.product_source.ProductMountRefusal

- Surface: ccf.graph.product_source.ProductMountRefusal
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: the typed mount refusal, deriving from
  `ccf.products.gates.GateRefusal` (the `AdmissionRefusal` derivation
  style), carrying the product's `tenant_id` and `schema_name` plus a
  `reason` field naming what was missing or invalid
- Why: the refusal-value discipline — a governance verdict a bare `except`
  could swallow is not a verdict; one gate vocabulary across the fabric

*Recorded by `15.2-10-product-source.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.product_source.GATE_PRODUCT_MOUNT

- Surface: ccf.graph.product_source.GATE_PRODUCT_MOUNT

- Why: a refusal an operator reads in a log names the gate that refused

- Why: the accepted-mode sets on the product class must reference the same

- Public surface: true

#### ccf.products.gates.AttestationRefusal

- Surface: ccf.products.gates.AttestationRefusal
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no typed refusal existed for a source whose attestation
  a product class does not accept
- After: derives from `GateRefusal` (the `AdmissionRefusal` precedent), so
  the `ServableProduct | GateRefusal` union of `run_gates` is unchanged;
  carries `failing_dimension: Literal["policy_binding", "lineage"]`,
  `source_mode`, and `accepted_modes` — the `PayloadTooLargeRefusal`
  diagnostic-pair style, naming the failing dimension per D-09
- Why: D-09 requires refusal as a returned value naming the failing

  base means no caller learns a new shape

#### ccf.products.gates.refuse_attestation

- Surface: ccf.products.gates.refuse_attestation
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `refuse_attestation(*, attestation, product_class) ->
  AttestationRefusal | None` — the one D-09 accepted-modes comparison,
  called by both enforcement layers (the engine pre-check and the
  `run_gates` gate path) so the two cannot drift into two readings of
  "accepted"; `None` (a pre-attestation record or a pre-phase reader)
  passes — the compatibility posture
