#### ccf.graph.backends.neo4j.Neo4jGraphStore

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.neo4j.Neo4jGraphStore
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no graph-native substrate existed; every traversal ran
  in SQL recursive CTEs or the in-process fallback
- After: `Neo4jGraphStore(uri=…, partition=…, database=…)` — connection and
  index owner over the official `neo4j` driver (extra-gated, lazy
  in-constructor import per the chroma_index precedent; constructing the
  store is the first moment the dependency is demanded). Physical model is
  adapter-owned (D-IMPL-34): claim-chain nodes carrying `claim_json`
  verbatim plus write-time projections of predicate-relevant fields,
  entity nodes joined by the fixed SDK-owned `CCF_EDGE` relationship type
  (artifact-authored CCF relationship types travel as a property and as
  driver parameters, never rendered into a Cypher pattern), timestamps as
  canonical RFC3339 UTC text (never Neo4j temporal types — the byte-drift
  trap). `partition` names one fabric's graph on a shared server — the
  Postgres schema role — so `graph_identity`
  (host/port/database/partition, never credentials — threat T-15.2-05)
  distinguishes fabrics per partition. Offers `reader`, `session()`,
  `close()`, and context management
- Why: D-06 ships all five adapters; 15.2-12 is the pushdown proof — a
  graph-native engine used as one
- Migration: new surface — none. `boot(neo4j_uri=…)` constructs the store;
  install with `pip install "ccf[neo4j]"`

*Recorded by `15.2-12-neo4j-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.neo4j.Neo4jGraphReader

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.neo4j.Neo4jGraphReader
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: a `GraphReader` over the driver, inheriting the whole shared read
  path from `BaseGraphReader`: five Cypher acquisition statements answer
  dict rows into the substrate-neutral materialization core
  (`ccf.graph.reader.materialize_view` — lineage-at-boundary,
  supersession-as-of, ordering and freezing are inherited, never
  re-implemented; 15.2-RESEARCH.md Pitfall 5). `_translate_query` answers
  whole queries natively (`GQ-32`): as-of / validity / policy predicates
  as parameterized Cypher over the write-time projections,
  `allowed_entities` as a driver list parameter (`GQ-30`), and the
  traverse family as an undirected variable-length `MATCH` whose path
  predicates enforce hop admissibility on every node and edge (`GQ-13`) —
  the one string-formatting site in the module is the validated-integer
  depth bound (Cypher quantifier bounds cannot be parameters; 15.2
  research Pitfall 7), bounded by `MAX_DEPTH_CEILING` with over-ceiling
  queries routed to the fallback under D-04. `as_of()` is the D-07
  degenerate-query derivation; `attestation` reports tier 1
- Why: the seam's strongest pushdown claim, demonstrated rather than
  asserted: traversal runs in Cypher, the survivor set constrains it
  natively, and the differential suite holds the answers byte-identical to
  the audited fallback
- Migration: new surface — none

*Recorded by `15.2-12-neo4j-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.neo4j.DEFAULT_PARTITION

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.neo4j.DEFAULT_PARTITION
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `"ccf"` — the partition a store attaches to when the caller does
  not say otherwise; a name, not a tuning value (CINV-01), mirroring
  `ccf.graph.backends.postgres.DEFAULT_SCHEMA`
- Why: partitions are what make "a fresh graph per boot" true on a
  Community-edition server with one database; the default names the
  single-fabric case
- Migration: new surface — none

*Recorded by `15.2-12-neo4j-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.neo4j.MAX_DEPTH_CEILING

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.neo4j.MAX_DEPTH_CEILING
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `100` — the mechanism safety bound on the ONE value the adapter
  inlines into Cypher text: the validated traversal-depth literal
  (quantifier bounds cannot be driver parameters — research assumption A2,
  re-verified live at execution). Deliberately NOT Control Plane
  configuration, and the contrast is documented on the constant: the
  admission payload ceiling is deployment-tunable policy; this bounds an
  injection surface of the adapter's own query text (CINV-01). An
  over-ceiling `depth_limit` is still answered — the translation declines
  and the memory-bounded fallback answers (D-04, `GQ-29`): the ceiling
  bounds Cypher text, never the query vocabulary
- Why: threat T-15.2-01/T-15.2-21 — the depth literal is the concentrated
  injection surface and the unbounded-pattern DoS vector; a named,
  documented, tested bound is the mitigation
- Migration: new surface — none

*Recorded by `15.2-12-neo4j-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.mongo.MongoGraphStore

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.mongo.MongoGraphStore
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no document substrate existed; the four shipped adapters
  were two SQL backends, an in-memory KV floor and a graph-native engine
- After: `MongoGraphStore(uri=…, partition=…, database=…)` — connection and
  index owner over the official `pymongo` driver (extra-gated, lazy
  in-constructor import per the chroma_index / neo4j precedent; constructing
  the store is the first moment the dependency is demanded). Physical model
  is adapter-owned (D-IMPL-34): six collections mirroring the relational
  layout, claim documents carrying `claim_json` verbatim plus write-time
  projections of predicate-relevant fields, timestamps as canonical RFC3339
  UTC text (never BSON dates — the byte-drift trap), compound unique indexes
  rendering the identity semantics, and a **doubled-edge traversal
  collection** — every edge stored forward AND reverse, because
  `$graphLookup` is directed while CCF traversal is undirected and two
  directed lookups do not compose (15.2-RESEARCH.md Pitfall 3). `partition`
  names one fabric's graph inside one database — the Postgres schema role —
  so `graph_identity` (host/port/database/partition, never credentials —
  threat T-15.2-05) distinguishes fabrics per partition. Offers `reader`,
  `collection()`, `scope()`, `close()`, and context management
- Why: D-06 ships all five adapters, and the aggregation pipeline is the
  substrate family neither SQL nor Cypher represents — the proof that the
  read seam is not shaped like a query language
- Migration: new surface — none. `boot(mongo_uri=…)` constructs the store;
  install with `pip install "ccf[mongo]"`

*Recorded by `15.2-13-mongo-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.mongo.MongoGraphReader

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.mongo.MongoGraphReader
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: a `GraphReader` over the database, inheriting the whole shared read
  path from `BaseGraphReader`: five acquisition reads answer dict rows into
  the substrate-neutral materialization core
  (`ccf.graph.reader.materialize_view` — lineage-at-boundary,
  supersession-as-of, ordering and freezing are inherited, never
  re-implemented; 15.2-RESEARCH.md Pitfall 5). MongoDB's dialect is a
  *document*, not a statement, so the five `SELECT_*` slots the base declares
  carry the **name** of each read and `_query` builds the filter from the
  typed positional parameters — strictly stronger than statement text for the
  T-15.2-01 surface, because there is no query language for a value to be
  mistaken for. `as_of()` is the D-07 degenerate-query derivation;
  `attestation` reports tier 1. The `_translate_query` hook answers the
  **whole** query (GQ-32) natively: `$match` filter documents for every record
  predicate, and — **stage 2, promoted after the corpus proved parity** — one
  `$graphLookup` over the doubled-edge collection for the traverse family,
  with hop admissibility and `allowed_entities` membership carried in
  `restrictSearchWithMatch`, `maxDepth = depth_limit - 1`, explicit seed
  re-inclusion, and the stage skipped entirely at `depth_limit=0`
  (15.2-RESEARCH.md Pitfall 3's three corrections plus the doubled layout)
- Why: the fifth adapter's read half, held to the same differential
  byte-identity bar as the other four
- Migration: new surface — none. Promotion is invisible to callers and to
  replay by construction: what was translated versus scanned is telemetry
  (`translated_answers` / `fallback_answers`), never a replay input (GQ-34)

*Recorded by `15.2-13-mongo-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.mongo.DEFAULT_PARTITION

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.mongo.DEFAULT_PARTITION
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `"ccf"` — the partition a store attaches to when the caller does not
  say otherwise; a name, not a tuning value (CINV-01), mirroring
  `ccf.graph.backends.postgres.DEFAULT_SCHEMA` and
  `ccf.graph.backends.neo4j.DEFAULT_PARTITION`
- Why: partitions are what make "a fresh graph per boot" true on a shared
  server; the default names the single-fabric case
- Migration: new surface — none

*Recorded by `15.2-13-mongo-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.mongo.DEFAULT_DATABASE

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.mongo.DEFAULT_DATABASE
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `"ccf"` — the database a store attaches to when neither the URI path
  nor the caller names one. Same posture as `DEFAULT_PARTITION`: a name,
  never a value
- Why: a MongoDB URI may or may not name a database, and a store that guessed
  differently per URI shape would make `graph_identity` — the key the
  single-writer boot check uses — depend on how the deployment happened to
  spell its connection string
- Migration: new surface — none

*Recorded by `15.2-13-mongo-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.fluree.FlureeGraphStore

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.fluree.FlureeGraphStore
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no RDF/JSON-LD substrate existed; the five shipped adapters
  were two SQL backends, an in-memory KV floor, a graph-native engine and a
  document store
- After: `FlureeGraphStore(url=…, partition=…, ledger=…, bearer_token=…)` —
  connection and ledger owner over plain HTTP, constructed on the core `httpx`
  pin with no extra to install. Fails at construction rather than at first read
  (`GET /health`, at the server root); discovers its API base once from
  `/.well-known/fluree.json` so it stays correct behind a differently-mounted
  server; and bootstraps the one CCF-owned ledger idempotently, treating `409
  Conflict` as success (D-03). Physical model is adapter-owned (D-IMPL-34):
  five node labels and one predicate, with every CCF relationship written as an
  **interposed `CcfEdge` node** joined to both endpoints by `CCF_LINK` — because
  on this engine a property-reading relationship variable sees only
  reifier-bundled edges, so a hop predicate stated on `r.prop` returns an empty
  reached set from a query that answers `200`. Timestamps are canonical RFC3339
  UTC text under a fixed **empty** `@context`, which is what keeps every literal
  an `xsd:string` and the as-of cut a plain text comparison. `graph_identity`
  is host/port/ledger/partition and carries neither the URL's userinfo nor the
  bearer token (threat T-15.2-05). Offers `reader`, `scope()`, `post_query()`,
  `post_update()`, `close()`, and context management
- Why: D-IMPL-88 rules Fluree v4 in as a tier-1 substrate, and this adapter is
  the Phase 15.2 no-refactor claim demonstrated on a real engine rather than
  asserted — a batched HTTP substrate landing behind the unchanged `_GraphTxn`
  seam is what that hedge was written for
- Migration: new surface — none. `boot(fluree_url=…)` constructs the store; no
  `pip install` extra is required

*Recorded by `15.3-02-fluree-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.fluree.FlureeGraphReader

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.fluree.FlureeGraphReader
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: a `GraphReader` over the ledger, inheriting the whole shared read path
  from `BaseGraphReader`: five acquisition reads answer dict rows into the
  substrate-neutral materialization core (`ccf.graph.reader.materialize_view` —
  lineage-at-boundary, supersession-as-of, ordering and freezing are inherited,
  never re-implemented). The dialect is openCypher over HTTP; rows are extracted
  from the cypher-json envelope, which upstream documents nowhere and which was
  therefore captured verbatim from a live `fluree/server:4.1.4` container before
  any adapter code existed. `_translate_query` **is** implemented and pushes
  every predicate family down to the server: the as-of cut, the validity
  window, the policy family as membership over the write-time materialized key,
  `allowed_entities` membership as a request list parameter, and the bounded
  undirected traversal as a walk over the interposed edge-node layout. An empty
  `allowed_entities` set is answered natively as the empty view with no round
  trip. **Four** named conditions decline to the fallback instead — an
  over-ceiling `depth_limit`, a policy family that cannot be encoded
  injectively, a rendered request body over `MAX_QUERY_BODY_BYTES`, and a walk
  refused by the server's path-enumeration cap (the one decline taken *after* a
  round trip). A decline is a change of path, never a change of answer: the
  fallback's result is byte-identical by construction and fully conformant
  under D-04 (`GQ-34`), which is why the `translated_answers` /
  `fallback_answers` telemetry is asserted separately rather than inferred from
  a passing differential suite. *(Corrected 2026-08-09, review finding WR-08:
  this entry previously said translation was deliberately not implemented,
  which was true of plan 15.3-02 and false of the shipped adapter — plans
  15.3-03 and 15.3-04 landed the translation and added no public name, so no
  later fragment superseded the claim.)*
- Why: the read seam is not shaped like a query language, and a sixth dialect
  answering the same five reads is the evidence
- Migration: new surface — none. Reached as `handle.reader`

*Recorded by `15.3-02-fluree-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.fluree.DEFAULT_PARTITION

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.fluree.DEFAULT_PARTITION
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `"ccf"` — the partition a store attaches to when the caller does not
  say otherwise. A name, never a tuning value (CINV-01); mirrors
  `ccf.graph.backends.postgres.DEFAULT_SCHEMA` and the neo4j/mongo twins. Two
  partitions in one ledger are two graphs, two `graph_identity` values and two
  independently bootable fabrics — which is what makes the single-writer check
  meaningful per fabric rather than per server
- Why: D-03 gives the deployment one CCF-owned ledger, so the partition is the
  whole of what distinguishes two fabrics on this substrate
- Migration: new surface — none. `boot(fluree_partition=…)` overrides it

*Recorded by `15.3-02-fluree-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.fluree.MAX_DEPTH_CEILING

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.fluree.MAX_DEPTH_CEILING
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `100` — a mechanism safety bound on the one value a translated
  statement inlines into query text, the validated traversal-depth literal
  (plan 15.3-04's promoted walk renders it; everything else travels as a
  request parameter).
  Declared on the `ccf.graph.backends.neo4j.MAX_DEPTH_CEILING` precedent, which
  D-GATE-15.2 checkpoint Q2 ratified as mechanism rather than a domain value. A
  `depth_limit` above the ceiling is still **answered**: the translation
  declines and the memory-bounded fallback answers (D-04)
- Why: the ceiling bounds generated Cypher text, never the query vocabulary —
  the distinction that keeps a safety bound from becoming a domain value
- Migration: new surface — none. Read-only mechanism bound

*Recorded by `15.3-02-fluree-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.fluree.MAX_QUERY_BODY_BYTES

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.fluree.MAX_QUERY_BODY_BYTES
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: `900_000` — a mechanism safety bound on the rendered query body,
  budgeted under this server's documented 1 MB `--max-query-size` limit. It has
  no analogue on the other five adapters because none of them serializes a query
  onto a wire with a size cap: `allowed_entities` travels as a parameter list,
  so a large survivor set is the shape that approaches it. Same posture and same
  ratified argument as `MAX_DEPTH_CEILING` — an over-budget query is still
  answered, by the fallback
- Why: a documented server limit that a legitimate query can reach must degrade
  to a byte-identical answer, never to a failure (D-04)
- Migration: new surface — none. Read-only mechanism bound

*Recorded by `15.3-02-fluree-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.BackendStore

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.BackendStore
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent as a public name — the same three members existed as
  `ccf.boot._Backend`, a private Protocol inside the composition root, so a
  third-party adapter had no declared shape to implement
- After: a public `Protocol` with `graph_identity`, `reader` and `close()` —
  what booting needs of a graph backend, whichever substrate it is. Protocol
  rather than a base class (CLAUDE.md §11): an adapter conforms structurally
  and inherits nothing, so nothing in the registry package can become a place
  adapter behaviour accumulates
- Why: an out-of-tree adapter has to implement the store contract, and a
  contract that lives behind a leading underscore in another module is not a
  contract anyone can implement
- Migration: none — new surface. `ccf.boot._Backend` was private and is
  unchanged by this plan

*Recorded by `15.4-02-registry.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.BackendRegistration

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.BackendRegistration
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — an adapter was reached by a hand-written branch in
  `ccf.boot._open_backend` that named it, imported it, and constructed both
  its store and its writer inline
- After: a frozen dataclass carrying `name`, `config_model` (deriving from
  `CCFContract`, so `extra="forbid"` is inherited rather than re-declared),
  `secret_fields`, `open_store` and `make_writer`. A record the registry
  *interprets*, never a base class an adapter subclasses (CLAUDE.md §6.4,
  §11) — an adapter is data to the registry
- Why: D-IMPL-93's per-adapter cost is only zero if an adapter can declare
  everything core needs to know about it without core naming it
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `15.4-02-registry.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.ResolvedBackend

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.ResolvedBackend
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: a frozen dataclass carrying the `registration` plus the `version` and
  `distribution` it came from. Both are **derived by the registry, never
  declared by the adapter** — from the declaring distribution's own metadata
  for a third-party adapter, and from this distribution's metadata for a
  built-in, with a documented fallback when that metadata is absent (a source
  checkout that was never installed)
- Why: a third-party adapter gets its replay pin for free and cannot mis-state
  it, because the two fields an audit reads are not fields the adapter writes
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `15.4-02-registry.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.AdapterPin

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.AdapterPin
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — no replay dimension recorded which adapter answered a
  decision, so "which substrate was this decided against" was unanswerable
  from a replay record
- After: a `CCFContract` with `name`, `version` and `secret_refs`. It has no
  field that can hold a resolved credential: `secret_refs` carries reference
  **names** only, which is what makes the credential path auditable without
  ever recording a credential. Plan 15.4-08 threads it into Decision Replay
  dimension 2 (whitepaper §7.10.1)
- Why: D-09 — the adapter in force is part of what a decision was made under,
  and a pin that could hold a value would be a pin nobody could safely persist
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `15.4-02-registry.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.ResolvedSecret

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.ResolvedSecret
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — a credential travelled as a bare keyword argument, so the
  name of the reference it was resolved from existed nowhere the fabric could
  read
- After: a `CCFContract` with `field`, `ref_name` and `value: SecretStr`. One
  object carries the config field a credential satisfies, the name of the
  reference it came from, and the resolved value, so a pin cannot record a
  reference the value did not come from and no caller has to keep two mappings
  in step. `SecretStr` renders as a fixed mask in `repr`, `str` and
  `model_dump_json`, and `ccf.contracts.canonical.canonical_bytes` refuses
  `SecretStr` outright — so a `ResolvedSecret` cannot be content-hashed into an
  artifact even by accident
- Why: D-08. Two parallel mappings — one of values, one of reference names —
  can drift; one object cannot
- Migration: none — new surface. An adapter's `open_store` still receives a
  mapping of config field to `SecretStr`, so the reference name travels only as
  far as the pin

*Recorded by `15.4-02-registry.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.UnknownBackend

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.UnknownBackend
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — an unregistered backend name was refused by a `Literal`
  membership failure that could say what was hard-coded, never what was
  installed
- After: a `ValueError` subclass whose message lists the names that *are*
  registered, sorted, plus the entry-point group an adapter becomes available
  through. `ValueError` because that is what a caller refusing a bad manifest
  already catches; the precise type is there for a caller that wants to tell
  this apart from a malformed connection mapping
- Why: D-12 — the refusal an adopter reads should name the deployment's actual
  adapter set
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `15.4-02-registry.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.DuplicateBackendName

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.DuplicateBackendName
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. Measured stdlib behaviour: entry-point names are not
  deduplicated across distributions and the mapping form returns the first
  match silently, so two distributions declaring one name resolved to whichever
  one happened to be first
- After: a `ValueError` subclass raised during **discovery**, naming both
  distributions and both versions and stating that the fabric does not choose
  between them. Because it fires in discovery, neither candidate is ever
  loaded
- Why: T-15.4-01 — a duplicate name is a broken deployment, and the same
  fail-closed posture `_open_backend` already takes on an ambiguous substrate
  selection
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `15.4-02-registry.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.ReservedBackendName

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.ReservedBackendName
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — nothing stopped an installed distribution declaring one of
  the SDK's own adapter names
- After: a `ValueError` subclass refusing an entry point that declares a name
  the SDK's own adapter package already holds, naming the declaring
  distribution. The SDK's adapters never travel the entry-point path at all, so
  this closes the shadowing hazard structurally as well as by refusal (D-16, on
  the D-IMPL-84 precedent that reserves the `ccf.` artifact-class prefix
  against manifest squatting)
- Why: a deployment whose adapter set depends on package discovery order is a
  deployment whose decisions cannot be reproduced
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `15.4-02-registry.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.declared_names

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.declared_names
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — the set of available backends was a six-name `Literal` on
  the deployment manifest, which could never say what a deployment had
  installed
- After: `declared_names() -> frozenset[str]`, the union of the two discovery
  sources. **Imports nothing** (D-05): built-in names are read off the adapter
  package's directory and third-party names are read from entry-point metadata
  — `.name`, `.value`, `.dist.name`, `.dist.version` — with `.load()` called
  never. Three refusals are applied first, so a name this function returns is a
  name `resolve` will honour
- Why: listing what a deployment has installed must not run arbitrary installed
  packages' module-level code
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `15.4-02-registry.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.resolve

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.resolve
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — `ccf.boot._open_backend` selected among six hand-written
  branches, each naming an adapter and importing it
- After: `resolve(name) -> ResolvedBackend`, the only function in the module
  that imports anything, and it imports exactly the one adapter the name
  selects. Discovery runs first, so a packaging conflict is refused before any
  third-party code runs. The loaded object is verified: it must be a
  `BackendRegistration` and its name must equal the name it was resolved under.
  A built-in write module discovered on disk but carrying no registration
  record is refused **by name**, never with an escaping `AttributeError`.
  Because discovery runs first, bad metadata from an adapter nobody selected
  fails every boot — the ruling below
- Why: one resolution path, one import site. The dynamic import here is
  invisible to an AST import lint, and the compensating control is confinement:
  exactly one `importlib.import_module` call site across `src/` and `runtime/`,
  asserted by test (T-15.4-03)
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `15.4-02-registry.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.validate_connection

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.validate_connection
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. Two separate refusals in `ccf.deployment.manifest`, driven by
  a hand-written mapping from backend kind to the one connection argument it
  needed, plus two more in `ccf.boot._open_backend` — four hand-maintained
  checks, none of which could be reused by a third-party adapter
- After: `validate_connection(registration, connection) -> CCFContract` — one
  function, validating a settings mapping against the adapter's own config
  model. It takes the *record* rather than a name, because every caller already
  holds one from `resolve` and taking a name would run discovery a second time.
  The refusal names the adapter (Pydantic names the model class, which is not
  the same thing), names the field as a dotted path, sorts its lines so two
  runs over one bad mapping produce one message, and reports every problem in
  one exception
- Why: T-15.4-05. A refusal built from a `ValidationError`'s own rendering
  embeds the value that was supplied, and one of those values is a credential —
  a misspelled connection key is precisely the case where a credential lands
  under a key nobody declared. Every line here is built from the error's field
  location and reason only; the supplied value is never read, and the refusal
  is raised outside the handler so the exception carries no chain at all —
  `raise ... from None` would only have suppressed the *printed* chain and left
  the `ValidationError` reachable on the object. Giving the refusal a
  single owner *is* the mitigation: three hand-rolled wrappers in the registry,
  the composition root and the manifest is how one of them ends up formatting
  `str(exc)`
- Migration: none — new surface

*Recorded by `15.4-02-registry.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.registry.InvalidBackendConfig

- Surface: ccf.graph.registry.InvalidBackendConfig
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — a bad connection mapping raised a bare `ValueError` from the
  manifest preflight, indistinguishable by type from an unknown backend name
- After: a `ValueError` subclass raised by `validate_connection`. Subclassing
  `ValueError` keeps every existing caller and every existing
  `pytest.raises(ValueError)` working, while a caller that wants to tell a
  malformed connection mapping apart from an unresolvable adapter name can have
  the precise type
- Why: the two failures have different fixes — one is a typo in a settings key,
  the other is a package that is not installed — and a type that cannot
  distinguish them makes a caller parse a message to find out
- Migration: none — new surface. Existing `except ValueError` handlers still
  catch it

*Recorded by `15.4-02-registry.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.sqlite_write.SqliteGraphWriter

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.sqlite_write.SqliteGraphWriter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: defined in `ccf.graph._writer`, a module invisible to the generated
  manifest by its leading-underscore segment — so the class was on no public
  name list
- After: defined in `ccf.graph.backends.sqlite_write`, beside the SQLite
  reader. The class did not change: same `BaseGraphWriter` subclass, same nine
  dialect `ClassVar` statements, same `_SqliteTxn` over a `sqlite3` connection,
  same `_SqlGraphTxn` shared adapter — only where it lives
- Why: D-01. While `ccf.graph._writer` imported all six adapters, importing the
  write path meant importing every substrate, and the registry seam D-IMPL-93
  specifies could not exist. Reads and writes in one module per backend was
  considered and rejected: it would force the single-writer lint to guard a
  *class* instead of a module, which is weaker and harder to check statically
- Migration: none. The module is import-restricted to `ccf.assembly` and
  `ccf.boot` by the `cinv02-graph-writer` lint; deployments reach the writer
  only through `boot(database_path=…)`. A deployment that had somehow named
  `ccf.graph._writer.SqliteGraphWriter` was already failing that lint

*Recorded by `15.4-03-write-modules.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.postgres_write.PostgresGraphWriter

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.postgres_write.PostgresGraphWriter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: defined in `ccf.graph._writer`, invisible to the generated manifest
- After: defined in `ccf.graph.backends.postgres_write`, beside the Postgres
  reader. Unchanged class: the same dialect statements (`%s` placeholders,
  `LEAST`/`GREATEST`, `seq` where SQLite reads `rowid`) driving the same
  `_SqlGraphTxn`, which stays with the neutral base because it is shared by
  both SQL adapters and would otherwise make one backend's module a dependency
  of another's
- Why: D-01, as above
- Migration: none — same import restriction, reached through
  `boot(postgres_dsn=…)`

*Recorded by `15.4-03-write-modules.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.memory_write.MemoryGraphWriter

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.memory_write.MemoryGraphWriter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: defined in `ccf.graph._writer`, invisible to the generated manifest
- After: defined in `ccf.graph.backends.memory_write`, beside the in-memory
  reader. Unchanged class: `_MemoryTxn` still implements the nine typed
  operations natively over the store's per-tenant dicts, with deep-copy
  snapshot-and-restore for all-or-nothing. The row types stay in the reader
  module and the mutations stay here, which is what keeps the reader's dicts
  read-only in practice as well as in principle
- Why: D-01, as above

  `boot(memory=True)`

#### ccf.graph.backends.neo4j_write.Neo4jGraphWriter

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.neo4j_write.Neo4jGraphWriter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: defined in `ccf.graph._writer`, invisible to the generated manifest
- After: defined in `ccf.graph.backends.neo4j_write`, beside the Neo4j reader,
  together with the ten parameterized Cypher write statements and
  `_neo4j_claim_projection`. Unchanged: `MERGE … ON CREATE` still reports
  creation through `nodes_created`, the per-partition `CcfMeta` counter still
  renders the arrival-order tiebreak, and the write-time projections are
  byte-for-byte what they were. Still extras-gated — the driver is demanded at

  fine
- Why: D-01, as above

  `boot(neo4j_uri=…)`

#### ccf.graph.backends.mongo_write.MongoGraphWriter

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.mongo_write.MongoGraphWriter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: defined in `ccf.graph._writer`, invisible to the generated manifest
- After: defined in `ccf.graph.backends.mongo_write`, beside the MongoDB
  reader, together with `_MongoTxn` and `_mongo_claim_projection`. Unchanged:
  the buffered `bulk_write(ordered=True)` batching, the read-before-buffer

#### ccf.graph.backends.fluree_write.FlureeGraphWriter

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.fluree_write.FlureeGraphWriter
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: defined in `ccf.graph._writer`, invisible to the generated manifest
- After: defined in `ccf.graph.backends.fluree_write`, beside the Fluree
  reader, together with the nine committed-state read statements, the node-kind
  and admit-prefix constants, `_fluree_claim_projection` and `_FlureeTxn`. The
  one-POST ground JSON-LD commit, the delete/insert replacement pair, and the
  per-key sequence arithmetic are unchanged. Needs no extra — the wire client
  is a core pin

  `boot(fluree_url=…)`

#### ccf.graph.backends.sqlite_write.SqliteConnection

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.sqlite_write.SqliteConnection
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. The SQLite adapter's one required connection argument was an
  entry in the `required` dict in `ccf.deployment.manifest._preflight_backend`
  (`"sqlite": "database_path"`), and the refusal for any *other* backend's
  argument supplied alongside it was a loop in the same function
- After: a frozen, closed-world model with one required field, `database_path`,
  annotated `Path` rather than `str` because Pydantic coerces a string to a
  `Path` and not the reverse while callers pass both. `extra="forbid"` is
  inherited from `CCFContract`, so an undeclared key is refused by the contract
  base rather than by a loop in core
- Why: D-07. An adapter should state its own requirements in its own module, so
  that adding a substrate costs no edit in a core file — the dependency-arrow
  reversal D-IMPL-93 asks for, applied to configuration as well as to code
- Migration: none. The name is new; nobody could have been using it

*Recorded by `15.4-03-write-modules.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.sqlite_write.REGISTRATION

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.sqlite_write.REGISTRATION
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent. `ccf.boot._open_backend` held this adapter's store
  construction and writer construction inline, in one branch of a six-branch
  cascade that named every substrate
- After: a module-level `ccf.graph.registry.BackendRegistration` naming this
  adapter (`sqlite`), its config model, its empty `secret_fields`, and its two
  private factories. `resolve("sqlite")` imports this module and reads it
- Why: D-07 and D-IMPL-93. This is the object that makes a built-in adapter and
  a third-party adapter the same kind of thing — the record is what an entry
  point in the `ccf.graph_backends` group points at
- Migration: none. The name is new; the supported path to a fabric is still
  `boot(...)` / `load_and_boot(...)`

*Recorded by `15.4-03-write-modules.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.postgres_write.PostgresConnection

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.postgres_write.PostgresConnection
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — `"postgres": "postgres_dsn"` in the core mapping, plus the
  schema default carried as a `boot()` keyword default
- After: a frozen, closed-world model with a required `dsn` and an optional
  `schema_name` defaulting to the adapter's own `DEFAULT_SCHEMA`. The field is
  `schema_name` and not `schema` because a field named `schema` shadows an
  attribute on Pydantic's `BaseModel` and emits a warning on every class
  construction; the store's keyword argument is unchanged and the mapping
  happens inside the adapter
- Why: D-07, as above
- Migration: none. The name is new

- After: the module-level record for the `postgres` adapter, with empty

#### ccf.graph.backends.memory_write.MemoryConnection

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.memory_write.MemoryConnection
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — and this adapter was the core mapping's one special case,
  `"memory": None`, which existed so the required-argument check could be
  skipped for it
- After: a model with **no fields at all**. Nothing is required, and any
  connection key whatsoever is refused by the inherited `extra="forbid"`. The
  special case disappears without a special case replacing it — which is the
  point: the empty model is the mechanism, not a placeholder
- Why: D-07. A special case in core is exactly the kind of substrate knowledge
  this phase moves out of core

- Before: absent — one branch of `ccf.boot._open_backend`'s cascade
- After: the module-level record for the `memory` adapter, with empty
  `secret_fields` and a store constructor that takes nothing
- Why: D-07 and D-IMPL-93, as above

#### ccf.graph.backends.neo4j_write.Neo4jConnection

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.neo4j_write.Neo4jConnection
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — `"neo4j": "neo4j_uri"` in the core mapping, with the

- After: a frozen, closed-world model with a required `uri` and an optional
  `partition`. The optional default stays `None` and resolves to the adapter's

#### ccf.graph.backends.neo4j_write.REGISTRATION

  driver
- After: the module-level record for the `neo4j` adapter. **The deferral is

- After: a frozen, closed-world model with a required `uri` and an optional
  `partition`, on the same `None`-default discipline as the Neo4j model
- Why: D-07, as above
- Migration: none. The name is new

#### ccf.graph.backends.mongo_write.REGISTRATION

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.mongo_write.REGISTRATION
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — one branch of `ccf.boot._open_backend`'s cascade, with the
  same deliberate import deferral as the Neo4j branch
- After: the module-level record for the `mongo` adapter, with the `pymongo`
  import still inside the store constructor and inside `_MongoTxn`

#### ccf.graph.backends.fluree_write.FlureeConnection

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.fluree_write.FlureeConnection
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — `"fluree": "fluree_url"` in the core mapping, with
  `fluree_bearer_token` a separate `boot()` keyword and a separate hand-written
  refusal for "a credential supplied for a backend nobody selected"
- After: a frozen, closed-world model carrying `url` and `partition` **only**.
  The bearer token is deliberately *not* a field, and the class docstring says
  so and says why, so nobody adds it later for convenience: Pydantic reports
  `input_value=` verbatim in a `ValidationError`, so a misspelled connection key
  beside a live token would put the token into the refusal message. The token
  is named in `secret_fields` and its value arrives in a separate mapping that
  is never validated
- Why: D-07, and threat T-15.4-05. The credential-free-message posture the
  adapter already takes (`_authorization` states that neither value appears in
  its refusal; `_graph_identity` drops userinfo) is extended to the one new
  surface that could have undone it
- Migration: none. The name is new

*Recorded by `15.4-03-write-modules.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.fluree_write.REGISTRATION

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.fluree_write.REGISTRATION
- Public surface: true
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — one branch of `ccf.boot._open_backend`'s cascade, which
  threaded `fluree_bearer_token` straight into the store constructor
- After: the module-level record for the `fluree` adapter, and the only one of
  the six whose `secret_fields` is non-empty: `frozenset({"bearer_token"})`.
  Its `open_store` reads that key out of the `secrets` mapping and unwraps the
  `SecretStr` at the call-site argument expression, so the plain value is never
  bound to anything that outlives the call
- Why: D-07 and D-IMPL-93. `secret_fields` is what lets the Control Plane know a
  credential is expected without a versioned document ever carrying one
- Migration: none. The name is new

*Recorded by `15.4-03-write-modules.md`.*

### Not public Python API

#### src/ccf/deployment/\_\_init\_\_.py

- Surface: src/ccf/deployment/__init__.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — there was no home for adopter-facing wiring, so an
  ergonomics helper would have landed wherever it was first needed
- After: an empty, algorithmic-tagged package root whose docstring records the
  SDK-extension citation (CLAUDE.md §6.4/§10), the CINV-03 reading
  ("algorithmic" is no model call, not no I/O), and the D-15A abstraction line
- Why: the phase's defining risk is an ergonomic helper silently deleting a
  whitepaper invariant. The package that will hold every helper states the line
  in its own docstring, and the standing guard has a real, non-vacuous target
  from the moment it is written rather than being retrofitted later
- Migration: none — the package exports nothing yet

*Recorded by `15-02-deployment-package.md`.*

#### cinv02-deployment-gates

- Surface: tools/lints/harness_imports.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: two rules under one AST walk — `cinv02-harness` and
  `cinv02-graph-writer`
- After: a third rule, `cinv02-deployment-gates`, refusing any import of
  `ccf.products.gates` from `ccf.deployment` or any of its submodules, in both
  the `import ccf.products.gates` and `from ccf.products import gates`
  spellings, with no allowlist
- Why: `run_gates` compares the product's declared
  `lineage.operators_invoked` against the engine's `engine_resolved`, and that
  comparison is a real check only because the two values arrive from two
  independent sources. A wiring helper that ran the gate is one step from
  deriving both from one input, at which point the gate compares a value against
  itself — green, tautological, and lineage strict equality (whitepaper §7.6,
  INV-CH7-47) deleted with nothing failing to say so. Gate execution stays in
  adopter code
- Migration: none for existing code — the rule denies one importer and the
  package it denies is new. Anything else may still name `ccf.products.gates`;
  the opposite-direction regression guard in
  `tests/lints/test_deployment_gates_ban.py` proves the ban did not widen

*Recorded by `15-02-deployment-package.md`.*

#### tools/measure_examples.py

- Surface: tools/measure_examples.py

  `docs/surface/examples/before/`; `removable` (mechanical wiring, which

  moves to `tests/`) are separate keys that are never summed

  numeric target, so the audit's honesty rests entirely on the phase-close
  figure being a re-measurement by the same code that produced the phase-start
  figure, and on relocation being reported apart from removal — D-12's stated
  risk is a target hit by moving lines rather than by removing work
- Migration: none — this is a repository tool, not part of the installed SDK.
  Plans that rewrite an example write an `after/` fragment beside the
  `before/` one; the `before/` baseline is written once, by this plan, and is
  never regenerated

#### docs/schemas/

- Surface: docs/schemas/
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — an adopter authoring an artifact document had no schema
  to point an editor at, and marshalling errors surfaced at boot
- After: one committed JSON Schema per entry in `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`
  (eleven today), each annotated `x-ccf-class-id`, regenerated by
  `make schemas` and held to the live models by a bytes-equality gate in the
  always-on `test` job that fails on drifted, missing, **and orphaned** files
- Why: D-11's generate-at-build posture has one standing exception, D-12's
  committed `openapi.json`, for contracts external consumers read. The
  schemas are the same case one step earlier: they are what adopters author
  *against*, in an editor, before anything runs, and a schema that exists
  only at build time cannot be the target of a `$schema` header in a
  checked-in document. Classes the SDK does not own get no committed schema
  — the SDK never invents one for a namespace that is not its own.
- Migration: run `make schemas` after adding or changing an `ArtifactClass`;
  a stale tree is a red build, not a silent difference

*Recorded by `15-12-schema-export.md`.*

#### make schemas

- Surface: make schemas
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: absent
- After: regenerates the committed `docs/schemas/` tree from
  `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`; `tools/dump_schemas.py --check` is the
  write-nothing form the gate uses
- Why: the single documented command surface (D-10) — every target CI runs, a
  developer runs the same way. The failure message names this target, because
  a failure nobody can act on is a failure that gets muted.
- Migration: none

*Recorded by `15-12-schema-export.md`.*

#### make schema-headers

- Surface: make schema-headers
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — nothing checked that a `$schema` header was *correct*
- After: `tools/check_schema_headers.py` verifies, per artifact document,
  that the header resolves from the document's **own directory** (as an
  editor resolves it, not as a repo-root-relative test would), that the
  target is a committed schema annotated with a real SDK class id matching
  its file name, that the named class's contract **actually accepts the
  document**, and that any deployment manifest in the directory declares the
  same `class_id` the header names. Auto-arming: a directory joins the
  checked set as soon as it carries its first header, so each example-rewrite
  plan gates its own headers at its own wave. `DIR=<path>` narrows it;
  `--require-all` is the tree-wide form for the phase close.
- Why: an editor hint has a failure mode no runtime has — it can be wrong for
  months and nothing breaks, because nothing reads it but the adopter's
  editor, which will happily annotate a document with confident nonsense from
  a real schema for the wrong class. Existence of the target is therefore not
  the property worth gating; correctness is. Validating through the SDK's own
  `load_artifact` makes the check exactly as strong as the validator the
  adopter's fabric runs at boot, and costs no new dependency.
- Migration: run `make schema-headers DIR=<your artifacts dir>` when adding
  headers

*Recorded by `15-12-schema-export.md`.*

#### examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/

- Surface: examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: no schema annotation of any kind
- After: each document carries an explicit
  `# ccf-schema: deferred(15-13) …` first line
- Why: **no SDK artifact class accepts any of these four documents today** —
  verified, not assumed — because each is a multi-artifact bundle and the
  contracts are `extra=forbid`. A `$schema` header on such a file would
  annotate every key belonging to the other artifacts as an error, so the
  "hint" would be a wall of false positives in the adopter's editor: worse
  than no header. Plan 15-13 splits these into typed per-class documents with
  a manifest and writes the real headers then, which is also when they become
  reviewable alongside the reshaped documents. The deferral is explicit,
  greppable, names the plan that closes it, and is *counted* — a document with
  neither a header nor a marker fails the gate, so nothing falls through
  silently.
- Migration: none — the markers disappear at 15-13

#### docs/schemas/ccf.agent-definition.json, ccf.prompt-artifact.json, ccf.task-definition.json, ccf.skill-catalog-entry.json, ccf.skill-grant.json, ccf.workflow-definition.json

- Surface: docs/schemas/
- Public surface: false
- Change type: added
- Before: absent — these six SDK-owned classes had no committed schema, so a hand-authored agent definition, prompt artifact or skill catalog entry had no honest `$schema` target and could only pass `make schema-headers` with a deferral marker
- After: committed JSON Schemas, emitted by `tools/dump_schemas.py` from a second set, `author_versioned_classes()`, alongside the `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` set and the envelope
- Why: all six carry D-IMPL-28 two-field versioning and register through `register_versioned`, whose D-05 bump check holds the author's `artifact_version` claim against content identity. `load_and_boot` registers through `register_typed`, which does not run that check — so they are deliberately **not** manifest-addressable, and adding them to `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` would have silently dropped the governance they exist to carry. They are nonetheless SDK-owned schemas an adopter authors by hand, so they are committed without becoming addressable, on the same reasoning that admitted the `DeclaredArtifact` envelope in 15-13
- Migration: point your agent definitions, prompt artifacts and skill catalog entries at their own committed schema, and keep registering them through `register_versioned` rather than through a manifest

*Recorded by `15-14-examples-01-02.md`.*

## Reshaped

Surfaces that still exist under the same name but whose shape moved. These are the entries an existing integration must read.

214 entries — 31 public Python surface, 183 not public Python API.

### Public Python surface

#### ccf.assembly.worker.EntityResolutionSpec

- Surface: ccf.assembly.worker.EntityResolutionSpec
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: a `CCFContract` with `entity_rules` and `relationship_rules` and no
  version field of any kind
- After: inherits `ccf.controlplane.artifact_base.VersionedArtifact`; `semver`
  is required and has **no default**
- Why: D-IMPL-77. The class's own docstring already called it "a Control Plane
  artifact, versioned like any other" — the field's absence made that sentence
  aspirational, and was one of exactly three things keeping the spec away from
  the `parse_artifact` seam. A **wrapper** holding `semver` beside the spec was
  rejected: `ccf.boot.boot` would then receive a wrapper where it expects a
  spec, changing `boot()`'s signature — the one contract D-05 committed to
  preserving. The wrapper trades a data change for a contract change, which is
  the more expensive of the two. No default, because a default lane is a version
  nobody chose and the registry's D-05 bump check would then compare content
  hashes against a lane the author never asserted
- Migration: **the content hash changes.** `semver` participates in
  `content_hash(spec)`, so every `resolution_spec_version` pin minted after this
  change differs from one minted before it, and every Decision Replay record
  carrying such a pin is pinned to a new value. Existing replay records remain
  valid against the artifact versions they name — nothing is invalidated and no
  record changes shape — but a re-registration of the *same* resolution rules
  now produces a different pin than it did before. Constructing an
  `EntityResolutionSpec` without `semver` is now a validation error: state the
  lane, and prefer reading it from the artifact rather than inlining one
  (CLAUDE.md §6.4)

*Recorded by `15-07-resolution-spec.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ManifestEntry

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ManifestEntry
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `path`, `key`, `class_id`, `role` — four required fields, and nothing said
  which registration path the entry travelled. `load_and_boot` called `register_typed`
  for every `role: register` entry unconditionally
- After: a fifth required field, `registration: RegistrationMode`, with **no default**
- Why: D-IMPL-85 (Dave, 2026-08-05). Either default would have the SDK deciding the
  *governance path* by silence for every entry that omitted it — a deployment would
  inherit or lose the D-05 bump check with no document saying so, which is exactly the
  convention-mistaken-for-enforcement failure this phase exists to close (T-15.1-02-01).
  The argument is a sibling of `role`'s, not a copy of it: `role`'s no-default reasoning
  is about a replay-dimension-2 *boundary*, and this one is about which governance
  obligations *run*. Rejected: optional-defaulting-to-`typed`, which is the cheap
  migration and the silent choice at once. The cost was accepted and measured with the
  ruling — 163 entries across 11 manifests plus the deployment test fixtures, one
  mechanical wave, and no external adopters to carry it (private repo, pre-1.0)
- Migration: **every manifest entry must now declare `registration`.** The mechanical
  edit is one line per entry, directly under `role`:

*Recorded by `15.1-02-registration-mode.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.pins.ArtifactPin

- Surface: ccf.deployment.pins.ArtifactPin
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `key`, `version`, `class_id` — the pin recorded what was in force and under
  which contract, and said nothing about how it got there
- After: a fourth required field, `registration: RegistrationMode`, carrying the mode
  the entry actually travelled
- Why: D-IMPL-83 (Dave, 2026-08-05). Bump evidence had to be reachable from the returned
  pins, and `ResolvedPins` is under an explicit no-growth constraint pinned by
  `tests/deployment/test_manifest.py::test_resolved_pins_has_exactly_one_public_method`.
  The reading adopted: that constraint forbids **methods that select**, not **fields
  that record** — `class_id` is already a recorded fact of the same kind — so the mode
  lands as a field and `ResolvedPins` gains no accessor. The audit log remains the
  **primary** evidence: `register_versioned` already audits every outcome with
  `action="register_versioned"` and `resolved=True|False`, which is how
  `test_the_author_versioned_definitions_kept_their_bump_check` reads it today. This
  field is the convenience for a reader holding the pins and no audit log. Rejected:
  audit-log-only, which leaves the evidence unreachable from the returned pins; and a
  `ResolvedPins` accessor, which contradicts the no-growth constraint directly
- Migration: **the pin set's content hash changes** — the field is additive to a
  `CCFContract`, so anything that recorded a pin-set hash before this plan records a
  different one after it. Determinism is unaffected:
  `test_two_loads_of_one_manifest_produce_an_identical_pin_set` still passes because
  both loads change identically. Code constructing an `ArtifactPin` directly must supply
  the field; code reading `version_for` is untouched

*Recorded by `15.1-02-registration-mode.md`.*

#### ccf.boot.boot

- Surface: ccf.boot.boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `boot(*, database_path=None, postgres_dsn=None, postgres_schema=…,
  registry, …)` — exactly one of `database_path` / `postgres_dsn` selected
  the substrate
- After: a `memory: bool = False` keyword joins the selector set; the
  exclusivity check now reads exactly one of {`database_path`,
  `postgres_dsn`, `memory=True`}, refused in the same
  the-fabric-does-not-infer-its-substrate ValueError style. The memory
  branch constructs `MemoryGraphStore` and a `MemoryGraphWriter`, so every
  `memory=True` boot is its own graph
- Why: explicit selection, never inference (the `_open_backend` posture) —
  the memory backend must be a statement, not a fallback a missing
  connection argument degrades to
- Migration: none for existing callers — the new keyword defaults to
  `False` and both existing selectors are untouched

*Recorded by `15.2-06-memory-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DeploymentManifest

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DeploymentManifest
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `backend: Literal["sqlite", "postgres"]` — every deployment
  manifest had to name a substrate the environment supplies a connection
  value for
- After: `backend: Literal["sqlite", "postgres", "memory"]`. A `memory`
  manifest needs no connection field and no environment argument — the D-08
  kind-only posture completed for a substrate with nothing to connect to.
  `load_and_boot` maps `backend: memory` to `boot(memory=True)`, and its
  preflight refuses a `memory` declaration accompanied by `database_path`
  or `postgres_dsn` exactly as it refuses the wrong backend's argument. The
  re-export `ccf.deployment.DeploymentManifest` reshapes identically. The
  committed `docs/schemas/ccf.deployment-manifest.json` regenerated
  (ERGO-12)
- Why: D-06 — a fabric that boots from a manifest must be able to name the
  always-on floor; SUBQ-08's memory leg runs through the same one call every
  other deployment uses
- Migration: none for existing manifests — both existing kinds are
  untouched; new manifests may declare `backend: memory` and supply no
  connection argument

*Recorded by `15.2-06-memory-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.projection.engine.ProjectionEngine

- Surface: ccf.projection.engine.ProjectionEngine
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: constructor `(reader, registry, acquirer)`; `execute()` performed
  one `reader.as_of()` everything-read and ran all operators in-process
- After: constructor gains keyword-only `survivor_cap: int | None = None`
  (D-10 — mechanism only; the value is Control Plane configuration, wired in
  plan 15.2-08) and a `last_survivor_set_sizes: tuple[int, ...]` telemetry
  property (per-execution, reset at `execute()` start, deliberately outside
  the replay record, report-only). `execute()` — public signature unchanged
  — now splits the plan and issues one `reader.query()` per pushable
  segment, re-applying each pushed segment's operators over its answer
  (`GQ-19`) and threading survivors as `allowed_entities` (`GQ-15`)
- Why: the D-IMPL-87 resolution's execution design: per-segment questions
  instead of the everything-read, with the resurrection defect closed at
  the seam and the cap decision Control-Plane-valued (D-10)
- Migration: none — every product, provenance chain, and gate outcome is
  byte-identical to the pre-segmentation path (pinned for both plan shapes
  in `tests/projection/test_segmentation.py`); constructing with the
  default `survivor_cap=None` preserves the no-cap posture

*Recorded by `15.2-07-read-intent-segmentation.md`.*

#### ccf.projection.engine.ExecutionResult

- Surface: ccf.projection.engine.ExecutionResult
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: no record of the substrate questions an execution asked
- After: gains sentinel-defaulted `executed_queries: tuple[GraphQuery, ...]
  = ()` — the issued queries in execution order, appended at each
  `reader.query()` call site (`GQ-20`); a cap firing is visible as an
  absent entry
- Why: plan 15.2-08 threads the per-round-trip `content_hash` pins from
  this field into the Decision Replay record (D-11) without reshaping the
  loop; the engine records what ran, never what the plan predicted
- Migration: none — the field defaults to the empty tuple; existing
  consumers of `ExecutionResult` are unchanged

*Recorded by `15.2-07-read-intent-segmentation.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.serve.serve

- Surface: ccf.replay.serve.serve
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `serve(product, record, replay_writer)` — append the record, then
  deliver
- After: gains keyword-only `query_pin_writer: QueryPinWriter | None = None`
  and `executed_queries: tuple[GraphQuery, ...] = ()`; when the seam is
  handed the executed queries their canonical bytes are persisted **before**
  the replay-record append, after an element-for-element consistency check
  against the record's `graph_query_sequence`
- Why: D-11's ordering commitment — no moment exists at which an appended
  record pins bytes the store does not hold (T-15.2-28)
- Migration: none — both parameters default to the pre-15.2-08 posture, so
  every existing call site is byte-for-byte unchanged; the governed path
  passes `execution.executed_queries` plus a `QueryPinWriter`

*Recorded by `15.2-08-replay-query-pins.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.ContentGenerationPath

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.ContentGenerationPath
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: dimension 5 recorded the plan, steps, product address, and the
  workflow step sequence; nothing recorded the substrate questions asked
- After: gains sentinel-defaulted `graph_query_sequence: tuple[str, ...] =
  NO_GRAPH_QUERIES` — one `content_hash` per issued `GraphQuery`, in
  execution order; a Similarity-bearing execution carries two pins, a
  cap-firing execution carries one — the record shows what ran
- Why: D-11 (D-IMPL-87 lineage) — replay recomputes the sequence and
  compares hashes; the pins derive from the engine's executed-queries record
  (`GQ-20`), never plan-side prediction (T-15.2-15)
- Migration: none — the sentinel default; stored records lacking the field
  rebuild to it and their stored content addresses still verify (D-IMPL-89)

*Recorded by `15.2-08-replay-query-pins.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ProductClassDeclaration

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ProductClassDeclaration
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the declaration carried no statement of which source-attestation
  modes the class accepts — every class implicitly consumed whatever source
  the deployment wired
- After: gains `accepted_policy_binding_modes: frozenset[PolicyBindingMode]
  = {"assembly-stamped"}` and `accepted_lineage_modes: frozenset[LineageMode]
  = {"graph-supplied"}` — unordered accepted-mode **sets** (not ordered
  minima; no strength ordering between `assembly-stamped` and
  `admission-verified` is invented), defaulting to the tier-1 posture;
  `docs/schemas/ccf.product-class.json` regenerated
- Why: D-09 — a product class must be able to demand provenance; the tier-1
  defaults make the demand every pre-attestation class already implicitly
  made, so consuming a tier-2 source becomes an explicit authored opt-in
  (deny-by-default) and nothing existing changes behavior
- Migration: none — existing YAML validates unchanged (the defaults);
  a class that should consume a mounted admitted product declares
  `accepted_policy_binding_modes: [assembly-stamped, admission-verified]`
  and `accepted_lineage_modes: [graph-supplied, product-carried]`

*Recorded by `15.2-11-attestation-enforcement.md`.*

#### ccf.products.gates.run_gates

- Surface: ccf.products.gates.run_gates
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the canonical three-gate sequence over
  `(candidate, engine_resolved, now, registry, product_class)`
- After: gains keyword-only `source_attestation: SourceAttestation | None =
  None` — the pinned attestation of the execution that produced the
  candidate (the governed path passes `execution.source_attestation`);
  when present and below the class's accepted modes the additive gate
  returns `AttestationRefusal` after the canonical three, and no
  `ServableProduct` exists. The three pre-existing gates' code, docstrings,
  order, and refusal precedence are byte-untouched; the return union is
  unchanged
- Why: the candidate contract cannot carry its source's attestation (a
  product does not attest its own source; the execution record does), so
  the pin arrives as the plan-anticipated keyword-only parameter with a
  `None` default — the only shape that leaves every pre-attestation call
  site byte-for-byte unchanged
- Migration: none for existing callers (`None` passes); the governed
  serving path adds `source_attestation=execution.source_attestation`

*Recorded by `15.2-11-attestation-enforcement.md`.*

#### ccf.projection.engine.ProjectionEngine

- Surface: ccf.projection.engine.ProjectionEngine

- Before: no pre-execute attestation surface; `execute()` did not record
  the source's attestation
- After: gains `check_source_attestation(*, product_class) ->
  AttestationRefusal | None` — the caller-visible pre-execute check: zero
  queries (it touches only the reader's `attestation` property), refusal
  as a returned value, the same `refuse_attestation` comparison the gate
  path runs. `execute()`'s signature and return annotation are
  byte-identical to plan 15.2-08's
- Why: the D-09 early layer — a below-modes source is refused before the
  first byte moves; the check is advisory in position but not in effect,
  because the unconditional pin plus the gate path make `ServableProduct`
  unconstructible for a below-modes source regardless
- Migration: none — new method; serving code calls it before `execute()`
  in the same match-composition the gates use

*Recorded by `15.2-11-attestation-enforcement.md`.*

  and the manifest Literal in-phase, each in its owning plan
- Migration: none for existing manifests — all three existing kinds are
  untouched; new manifests may declare `backend: neo4j` and supply
  `neo4j_uri` from the environment

*Recorded by `15.2-12-neo4j-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.boot.boot

- Surface: ccf.boot.boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `boot(*, database_path=…, postgres_dsn=…, postgres_schema=…,
  memory=…, neo4j_uri=…, neo4j_partition=…, mongo_uri=…, mongo_partition=…,
  registry, …)` — five selectable backends
- After: two keyword-only parameters added, `fluree_url: str | None = None` and
  `fluree_partition: str | None = None`, and the exactly-one exclusivity check
  now counts six selectors and names `fluree_url (Fluree v4)` in its refusal.
  Unlike the neo4j and mongo branches, the Fluree branch imports its backend at
  **module top**: its wire client is a core pin, so the import shape is the
  Postgres one
- Why: selection is by explicit argument and never by inference from the shape
  of a string — a fabric that guessed its substrate could boot against a
  development file in production and still pass every test. Adding a substrate
  is therefore adding one more member of an existing enumeration
- Migration: none — both parameters default to `None` and every existing call
  is unaffected. A Fluree deployment passes `boot(fluree_url=…)`

*Recorded by `15.3-02-fluree-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.manifest.load_and_boot

- Surface: ccf.deployment.manifest.load_and_boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `load_and_boot(manifest_path, *, registry, database_path=…,
  postgres_dsn=…, neo4j_uri=…, mongo_uri=…, tenant_id=…)`
- After: one keyword-only parameter added, `fluree_url: str | None = None`,
  threaded to both `_preflight_backend` and `boot`. Preflight refuses a
  declared `fluree` backend with no URL **before any artifact is parsed or
  registered**, and refuses a URL supplied alongside a different declared
  backend
- Why: the D-08 kind-only posture — the manifest names the substrate, the
  environment supplies how to reach it, and the two are reconciled loudly
  before any Control Plane state exists. A Fluree URL can carry userinfo and the
  deployment may also hold a bearer token, so neither may reach a
  content-hashed audit artifact (threat T-15.2-05)
- Migration: none — the parameter defaults to `None`. A manifest declaring
  `backend: fluree` must supply `fluree_url` at the call site

*Recorded by `15.3-02-fluree-backend.md`.*

  diff is claimed explicitly rather than relying on a module-granular match
- Migration: none — identical to `ccf.controlplane.artifacts.DeploymentManifest`

*Recorded by `15.3-02-fluree-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.boot.boot

- Surface: ccf.boot.boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: sixteen keyword arguments, eleven of them per-database
  (`database_path`, `postgres_dsn`, `postgres_schema`, `memory`, `neo4j_uri`,
  `neo4j_partition`, `mongo_uri`, `mongo_partition`, `fluree_url`,
  `fluree_partition`, `fluree_bearer_token`), with the substrate selected by
  asking which of them was not `None`. Omitting all of them selected the
  development substrate by default
- After: eight keyword arguments — `backend`, `connection`, `secrets`,
  `registry`, `resolution_spec_key`, `resolution_spec_version`,
  `policy_bundle_key`, `policy_bundle_version`. `backend` is required and has
  no default; `connection` is a mapping validated against the resolved
  adapter's own contract; `secrets` is a mapping of config field to
  `ccf.graph.registry.ResolvedSecret`. No parameter names a database
- Why: the composition root named all six substrates, so adding a seventh cost
  an edit in core and a substrate name in core was unavoidable. After
  D-IMPL-93 an adapter declares itself and `boot` resolves the one a deployment
  named, which is what lets a third-party adapter ship as a separate installable
  distribution with no line changing in this repository. `backend` is required
  rather than defaulted because a fabric that could infer its own substrate
  could boot against a development store in production and still pass every
  test
- Migration: replace the per-database keyword with `backend=` plus
  `connection=`, using the table above. Two worked cases.

*Recorded by `15.4-05-boot.md`.*

#### ccf.boot.FabricHandle

- Surface: ccf.boot.FabricHandle
- Public surface: true
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: constructed with `reader`, `worker`, `registry` and `shutdown`, and
  exposing the first three. It carried capabilities and no state a caller could
  misuse
- After: additionally constructed with `adapter` and exposing
  `handle.adapter`, a `ccf.graph.registry.AdapterPin` carrying the adapter's
  name, its version, and the sorted names of the credential references in force
- Why: from D-IMPL-93 onward the adapter behind the graph seam can be
  third-party code, so which adapter answered and at which version is part of
  what produced the answer — whitepaper §7.10.1 dimension 2. The pin has no
  field that can hold a credential: `secret_refs` carries reference *names*,
  which is what makes the credential path auditable without recording a
  credential. `FabricHandle` still has no writer attribute, private or
  otherwise
- Migration: none for a reader. A caller that constructs a `FabricHandle`
  directly — which nothing in the SDK does outside `boot` — must now pass
  `adapter`

*Recorded by `15.4-05-boot.md`.*

### Not public Python API

#### tools/lints/__main__.py

- Surface: tools/lints/__main__.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `LINTED_TREES = ("src", "runtime", "examples")` and
  `RULE_TREE_SCOPE["no-hardcoding"] = ("src", "runtime", "examples")` — the
  No-Hardcoding Rule did not reach test modules
- After: both tuples gain `"tests"`. Two edits, not one: `run()` iterates
  `LINTED_TREES` and *then* filters each rule by `RULE_TREE_SCOPE`, so a tree
  named only in the scope map is never walked and the widening would be a
  no-op. `harness-imports`, `algorithmic`, and `orchestration-imports` stay
  `("src", "runtime")` — test modules exercising the harness adapters import
  harness SDKs, and CLAUDE.md §7.3 words that invariant over core
- Why: D-IMPL-78 (Dave, 2026-08-04). D-09 relocates the example CI assertions
  into `tests/`, and those assertions carry domain expectations read from
  `expected:` artifact blocks. Without this scope the relocation would move
  domain values out of a structural invariant's reach with every CI gate still
  green — a CI-invisible weakening arrived at as a side effect of an
  ergonomics change. The hole is closed before any relocation happens
- Migration: downstream forks that add test modules with inline domain
  literals — prompt-shaped strings, numeric threshold tables, direct `Agent` /
  `Task` / `Workflow` construction — will now see CI findings under
  `tests/`. The fix is the same one `/examples` already takes: read the
  expected value from the artifact the test asserts about, and construct
  agents through the Agent Factory from fixture artifacts. The exemption
  surface for a whole tree is this config file, never an allowlist entry

*Recorded by `15-04-lint-scope.md`.*

#### tools/lints/no_hardcoding.py

- Surface: tools/lints/no_hardcoding.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `cinv01a-prompt-shape` scored every prose-shaped literal that
  survived the SQL/URI/short-string filters. Developer-facing diagnostics were
  indistinguishable from prompts to the rule, so a multi-sentence CI failure
  message naming its own fix scored as a system instruction
- After: `is_developer_diagnostic()` runs before scoring. A literal that
  carries at least one `DIAGNOSTIC_MARKERS` signal — a backticked command, a
  repo-relative filename, a `callable()` reference — and does not
  `addresses_a_reader()` (role-preamble opener, second-person pronoun, or
  template-brace density) is a diagnostic, and is not scored
- Why: Dave's ruling of 2026-08-04, recorded in `15-03-audit-baseline.md` and
  binding on this plan: resolve the two prose-diagnostic false positives by
  refining the rule, **not** by adding allowlist entries. Two allowlist entries
  today becomes friction on every future diagnostic message, and this phase's
  conventions actively encourage diagnostics that name their own fix (15-01
  ships `test_the_drift_message_names_its_own_fix` asserting exactly that).
  The distinction is mechanically detectable: a prompt is a string sent to a
  model, a diagnostic is a string shown to a developer, and the old rule
  detected "long English string literal" — a proxy for the wrong thing
- Migration: a CINV-01a finding that was previously suppressed by an allowlist
  entry on a diagnostic message can have that entry deleted; none existed in
  this repo, and none were added. A genuine prompt is unaffected — the reader
  test means it cannot escape by mentioning a filename, proven by
  `test_a_prompt_wearing_diagnostic_markers_is_still_flagged`

*Recorded by `15-04-lint-scope.md`.*

#### src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py

- Surface: src/ccf/controlplane/artifacts.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the module docstring and the `DeclaredArtifact` class docstring both
  named "operator parameter sets" — unqualified — as a shape whose schema belongs
  to a deployment
- After: both say **deployment-authored** operator parameter sets, and both state
  the rule D-01 actually states: the SDK owns the schema of what it ships and
  never invents one for what it does not. The four categories the SDK ships have
  typed classes; everything else takes the open envelope
- Why: D-01. The unqualified claim was no longer true once this plan shipped the
  four typed classes, and amending only one of the two sites would leave the
  module contradicting itself. The policy-bundle rationale is kept intact — it
  was sound for policy bundles and remains the reason `DeclaredArtifact` exists;
  what changed is its scope, not its truth. mkdocstrings publishes both
  docstrings, so Phase 16's rendered reference changes with this edit, which is
  why it is a recorded surface change and not a comment tidy
- Migration: none — no name, signature or behavior changes; the docstrings are
  the deliverable

*Recorded by `15-08-operator-parameters.md`.*

#### ccf.signal-batch

- Surface: ccf.signal-batch
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the event document used flat keys the SDK contract does not declare —
  `source_system_id:` for what the contract nests as `source: {system_id: ...}`,
  and `payload:` for what the contract calls `raw_payload:`. `tenant_id` was not
  in the event document at all; it was read from `ontology.yaml`
- After: the document is in the SDK's own envelope vocabulary — nested
  `source: {system_id: ...}` and `raw_payload:` — with `tenant_id` declared once
  on the batch, alongside the batch's `semver` lane
- Why: the translation between the two vocabularies is **removed rather than
  relocated**. The SDK does not grow a second field vocabulary for a shape it
  already owns, and D-15B permits the churn. The change is to the *container*
  keys only: `raw_payload`'s contents stay opaque to L1 and its keys stay the
  source system's vocabulary, exactly as `SignalEnvelope`'s docstring states
  (T-15-09-04, accepted). This entry is `Public surface: false` because an
  artifact-document format change can never appear in an `__all__`-derived
  manifest diff — see `docs/surface-changes/README.md`
- Migration: transform each event document mechanically —
  1. `source_system_id: X` becomes `source:` / `  system_id: X`
  2. `payload:` becomes `raw_payload:` (contents unchanged)
  3. add `tenant_id:` at the batch root, carrying the value previously read from
     the ontology document, and add `tenant_id:` on each envelope — the batch
     refuses a disagreement rather than stamping over one
  4. add the artifact's own `semver:` lane at the document root

*Recorded by `15-09-signal-perspective-productclass.md`.*

- After: `ccf.resolution-spec` carrying `semver`, `entity_rules` and `relationship_rules` and nothing else

#### examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/operator-parameters-similarity.yaml

- Surface: examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/operator-parameters-similarity.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `operator_parameters.similarity:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, with `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class` in `ontology.yaml` and `index_version` alongside the query
- After: a `ccf.operator-parameters.similarity` document carrying query, threshold, ranked classes and the corpus attribute map; the index version moves out
- Why: the corpus attribute map governs index *construction* and belongs with the artifact that declares it. The index version does not: it identifies a corpus state at a named boundary, not a parameter set, so it is supplied to the builder per run

#### examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/product-class.yaml

- Surface: examples/00-quickstart/artifacts/product-class.yaml
- Public surface: false

- Before: the `product_class:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, carrying `plan_id` and `product_ttl_seconds` and declaring no operator set
- After: a `ccf.product-class` document declaring `required_operator_keys` and `max_ttl_key`; `plan_id` and `product_ttl_seconds` move to the deployment's own governance document

#### examples/02-skilling/artifacts/decision.yaml

- Surface: examples/02-skilling/artifacts/decision.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `decision:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`
- After: a `DeclaredArtifact` document that is **not** named by the manifest, read one line before it
- Why: this deployment injects its decision instant as the Control Plane registry's clock so the audited grant lifecycle is reproducible — and the registry is a constructor argument to `load_and_boot`, so the instants must be known first. It goes through the same `load_artifact` seam either way
- Migration: if you inject a clock, read the document that supplies it before calling `load_and_boot`

*Recorded by `15-14-examples-01-02.md`.*

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/resolution-spec.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/resolution-spec.yaml
- Public surface: false
