- Before: `ontology.yaml`, carrying `schema_version`, `artifact_version`, `tenant_id`, `entity_rules`, `relationship_rules`, `classification_attribute_by_class` and `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class`
- After: `ccf.resolution-spec` carrying `semver`, `entity_rules` and `relationship_rules` and nothing else
- Why: D-03 makes the class closed-world over resolution rules only; the two attribute maps moved to the operator-parameters documents that consume them, and `tenant_id` to the manifest
- Migration: rename the file, replace both root version keys with a single `semver`, move `tenant_id` to the manifest, and move each attribute map to its operator's parameters document

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/events.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/events.yaml

- After: a `ccf.signal-batch` with `semver`, `tenant_id`, and envelopes using nested `source: {system_id: ...}` and `raw_payload:`
- Why: the document adopts the SDK's own envelope vocabulary, so the loader has no translation to perform and none to drift
- Migration: nest `source_system_id` under `source: {system_id: ...}`, rename `payload:` to `raw_payload:`, add `tenant_id` at batch level and on each envelope

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/operator-parameters-similarity.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/operator-parameters-similarity.yaml

- Before: the `operator_parameters.similarity:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, including `index_version`, with `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class` in `ontology.yaml`
- After: a `ccf.operator-parameters.similarity` document carrying the query, the threshold, the ranked classes and the corpus attribute map — **without** `index_version`
- Why: one artifact, one consumer; and the corpus's version identity is a property of graph state at a named boundary, not of these parameters (INV-CH7-27), so it moved to `product-governance.yaml` and is passed to `build_operator_registry`
- Migration: lift the parameters to the root, move the corpus attribute map in beside them, add `semver`, and move `index_version` to wherever your deployment names its boundary

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/product-class.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/product-class.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `product_class:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, with no declared operator set — `run.py` passed `operators.keys()` to the projection plan
- After: a `ccf.product-class` document carrying `max_ttl_key` and `required_operator_keys`, and deliberately no `max_ttl_version`
- Why: `required_operator_keys` is the authored declared side of Gate 2 (INV-CH7-47); a plan returning the registry's own keys made the gate compare a value against itself. `max_ttl_version` is minted by *registering* the ceiling, so an artifact declaring it would be authoring a hash of something it does not contain
- Migration: lift the block to the root, add `semver`, add `max_ttl_key` and `required_operator_keys`, drop `plan_id` and `product_ttl_seconds` (they moved to the governance document), and stop passing the registry's keys to your plan

*Recorded by `15-15-examples-03-07.md`.*

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/product-governance.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/product-governance.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `max_ttl_seconds`, `product_ttl_seconds`, `product_class.plan_id`, `operator_parameters.similarity.index_version` and `artifacts.replay_record_class`, scattered through `controlplane.yaml`
- After: one `DeclaredArtifact`-envelope document, `class_id: atlas-commerce.product-governance`, carrying the four governance values the SDK declines to own
- Why: these are values the SDK deliberately holds no default for; grouping them in one deployment-authored document keeps each one's independence visible (the TTL ceiling and the product TTL are separately authored, which is what leaves Gate 3 a real comparison)
- Migration: collect the values under `declaration:` and name the document `role: register`

*Recorded by `15-15-examples-03-07.md`.*

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/decision.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/decision.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `decision:` and `instruction_parameters:` blocks inside `controlplane.yaml`
- After: one `DeclaredArtifact`-envelope document, `class_id: atlas-commerce.decision-inputs`, **`role: load`**
- Why: what instant one run asked about, and what parameters it bound, are not part of what the fabric was *permitted to do* — registering them would put them in replay dimension 2, where re-running at a different boundary would read back as a governance change
- Migration: collect the values under `declaration:` and name the document `role: load`

*Recorded by `15-15-examples-03-07.md`.*

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/expected.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/expected.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `expected:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, asserted against inside `run.py`
- After: one `DeclaredArtifact`-envelope document, `class_id: atlas-commerce.expected-product`, `role: load`, read by `tests/examples/test_harness_portability_output.py`; it additionally declares `harness_leg_count`
- Why: an expectation is not a permission, so it has no business in Control Plane state; and the declared leg count closes the vacuity hole in "all legs agree", which is trivially true of one leg
- Migration: collect the values under `declaration:`, name the document `role: load`, and read them from `booted.artifacts` in your test rather than inlining them

*Recorded by `15-15-examples-03-07.md`.*

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/harnesses.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/harnesses.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: three top-level per-harness blocks, read with `yaml.safe_load`
- After: one `DeclaredArtifact`-envelope document, `class_id: atlas-commerce.harness-bindings`, **`role: load`**, with the three blocks under `declaration:`
- Why: a harness binding is how a harness is stood up *outside* the fabric, so it is not a fabric permission and has no business in replay dimension 2 — which harness ran is recorded per leg on the `HarnessTrace` instead
- Migration: nest the per-harness blocks under `declaration:`, add `semver` and `class_id`, name it `role: load`, and pass `artifact.declaration` where you passed the parsed document

*Recorded by `15-15-examples-03-07.md`.*

- After: one `DeclaredArtifact`-envelope document, `class_id: atlas-commerce.live-runner`, with both blocks under `declaration:`
- Why: it needed an honest `$schema` header like every other document in the directory. It is deliberately **not** a manifest entry: it configures the opt-in live legs, which are excluded from `make ci`, so naming it in the manifest would make the CI leg parse configuration for a mode it never enters
- Migration: nest the two blocks under `declaration:`, add `semver` and `class_id`, and load it with `load_artifact` rather than `yaml.safe_load`

*Recorded by `15-15-examples-03-07.md`.*

#### examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/agents/agent-definition.yaml

- Surface: examples/03-harness-portability/artifacts/agents/agent-definition.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `agents/order_insights.yaml`, one file carrying three things — a `keys:` block, a `referenced_artifacts:` block with two artifacts inlined, and the definition itself under `agent_definition:`
- After: the `ccf.agent-definition` at the document root, with a real `$schema` header; the keys moved to `agent-registration.yaml` and the two referents to `agents/policy-regime.yaml` and `agents/debt-profile.yaml`
- Why: one class, one document — a file carrying three classes cannot be annotated, because each header marks the others' keys as errors. Inlining the referents also gave one document several version identities, which is what `VersionedRef` exists to prevent
- Migration: lift `agent_definition:` to the root, move the `keys:` block to a registration document, and split each `referenced_artifacts` entry into its own envelope document whose `semver` equals the version its ref pins

#### examples/07-artifact-store-mcp/artifacts/product-governance.yaml

- Surface: examples/07-artifact-store-mcp/artifacts/product-governance.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: `max_ttl_seconds`, `product_ttl_seconds`, `product_class.plan_id`, the similarity `index_version`, and the whole `artifacts:` block (replay record class, product class and name, policy-bundle class and name), scattered through `controlplane.yaml`
- After: one `DeclaredArtifact`-envelope document, `class_id: nordhaven-logistics.product-governance`, carrying all of them — with the artifact class/name values renamed `product_artifact_class`, `product_artifact_name`, `policy_bundle_artifact_class`, `policy_bundle_artifact_name`
- Why: these are values the SDK declines to own. Grouping them here also makes the store claim legible: both runs read the same class and name values from this same document, which is what makes "the same artifacts landed at the same versioned URIs" a statement about the two *stores* rather than about two configurations
- Migration: collect the values under `declaration:`, rename the artifact class/name keys, and name the document `role: register`

*Recorded by `15-15-examples-03-07.md`.*

- Migration: collect the values under `declaration:` and name the document `role: load`

#### examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/operator-parameters-similarity.yaml

- Surface: examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/operator-parameters-similarity.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `operator_parameters.similarity:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, with `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class` in `ontology.yaml` and `index_version` alongside the query
- After: a `ccf.operator-parameters.similarity` document carrying query, threshold, ranked classes and the corpus attribute map; the index version moves out
- Why: the corpus attribute map governs index *construction* and belongs with the artifact that declares it. The index version does not: it identifies a corpus state at a named boundary, not a parameter set
- Migration: move `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class` out of the ontology; pass `similarity_index_version` to `build_operator_registry`

#### examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/product-class.yaml

- Surface: examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/product-class.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `product_class:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, carrying `plan_id` and `product_ttl_seconds` and declaring no operator set
- After: a `ccf.product-class` document declaring `required_operator_keys` and `max_ttl_key`; `plan_id` and `product_ttl_seconds` move to the deployment's own governance document
- Why: `required_operator_keys` is the authored declared side of the lineage gate. Without it the projection returned the operator registry's own keys — `PaymentNetworkPlan(required=operators.keys())` — and Gate 2 compared a value against itself
- Migration: add `required_operator_keys` naming the operator keys your projection depends on, and read it in your projection instead of the registry's keys

*Recorded by `15-16-example-08.md`.*

- After: one deployment-authored document in the `DeclaredArtifact` envelope carrying all of them
- Why: these are values the SDK deliberately declines to own; the TTL ceiling and the product's own lifetime in particular stay two separately authored numbers, which is the whole reason Gate 3 is a check
- Migration: collect the equivalents from your Control Plane bundle into a declared document

#### examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/policy-bundle.yaml

- Surface: examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/policy-bundle.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `policy_bundle:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, registered as a bare mapping under the key `meridian-bank/policy-bundle/financial-crime-analyst`
- After: a document in the open `DeclaredArtifact` envelope — `semver`, `class_id`, `declaration` — registered under the short manifest key `policy-bundle`
- Why: the SDK never invents a policy-bundle schema, but it does own the envelope, and the envelope is what gives a deployment-authored document a version lane and a checkable class identity
- Migration: wrap your bundle in `declaration:` and add `semver` and `class_id`. **Note that both the content and the Control Plane key change, so the pinned version changes and any recorded replay reference over this bundle is regenerated** — see the narration note below

*Recorded by `15-16-example-08.md`.*

- After: its own envelope document, named by the manifest under the key the agent definition's versioned-URI ref resolves against
- Why: `agent.yaml` was three artifact classes in one file, and `run.py` carried a loop that registered artifacts out of another artifact's body. The ref and the registration are now two independent statements of one fact
- Migration: lift each `referenced_artifacts` entry into its own document and give the manifest an entry whose `key` is exactly the string the ref names

#### examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/expected_output.txt

- After: byte-compared by `tests/examples/test_e2e_demo_output.py`; **one line regenerated** — the Stage 6 `replay record` content address
- Why: the reshape changed the policy bundle's content (it now carries the envelope's `semver`/`class_id`/`declaration`) and its Control Plane key (`policy-bundle` rather than the long namespaced one). Both land in the replay record's dimension-2 binding, so the record's own content hash moves. No other line of the transcript changes, and the regeneration is a reviewed event under this plan's blocking checkpoint
- Migration: none for adopters; if your own transcript pins a replay address, expect to regenerate it when any registered artifact's content or key changes

- After: a `ccf.agent-definition` document whose **root is the artifact**, annotated with that class's committed schema

#### examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/skill-dispatch.yaml

- Surface: examples/08-e2e-demo/artifacts/skill-dispatch.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `grant:`, `actors:` and `dispatch:` blocks inside `skills.yaml`
- After: one deployment-authored `role: load` document in the `DeclaredArtifact` envelope, carrying `grant_request`, `requesting_actor` and `dispatch`
- Why: **this is deliberately not a `ccf.skill-grant`, despite that class having a committed schema.** `SkillGrant` requires `agent_definition_hash`, which is derived identity computed by mechanism at request time — a hash is never an artifact value, so a standalone document of that class cannot be authored and a `ccf.skill-grant` header would be one its own class rejects. What *is* authorable is the grant request, and `run.py` completes it by supplying the hash
- Migration: rename your `grant:` block to `grant_request:` inside a declared document, and leave the derived hash out

*Recorded by `15-16-example-08.md`.*

- Before: the `keys:` blocks at the top of `agent.yaml` and `prompt.yaml`
- After: one deployment-authored `role: load` document naming both Control Plane keys
- Why: a key is where a document is *filed*, not part of what it says, and the author-versioned documents' roots had to become their artifacts. The skill catalog entry's key is deliberately absent — `SkillCatalog` derives it from the entry's own tenant and name, so authoring one would be a second, drifting statement of a value mechanism already owns
- Migration: collect the keys your driver reads into a declared document

- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `keys:` blocks scattered across `agent.yaml`, `prompt.yaml`, `task.yaml`, `workflow.yaml`, `hitl.yaml` and `scope_mapping.yaml`
- After: one deployment-authored `DeclaredArtifact`, `role: load`, naming where each driver-registered document files
- Why: a key is where a document is FILED, not part of what it says — and once each document's root must BE its artifact, there is nowhere else for the key to live. `role: load` because putting the filing scheme in force would content-hash it into replay dimension 2 alongside the artifacts it files
- Migration: gather the keys into one document and read them off `booted.artifacts`

*Recorded by `15-17-example-06.md`.*

#### examples/06-decision-replay/artifacts/skill-dispatch.yaml

- Surface: examples/06-decision-replay/artifacts/skill-dispatch.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `grant:`, `callable:`, `dispatch:` and `actors:` blocks inside `skills.yaml`
- After: one deployment-authored `DeclaredArtifact`, `role: load`, whose `grant_request` is the authorable half of a `ccf.skill-grant`
- Why: `SkillGrant` requires `agent_definition_hash`, which is derived identity computed at request time — a hash is never an artifact value, so a standalone `ccf.skill-grant` document cannot be authored even though that class has a committed schema. `role: load` because the ledger audits the grant independently when it admits it
- Migration: author the request, let `run.py` supply the hash

*Recorded by `15-17-example-06.md`.*

#### examples/06-decision-replay/artifacts/hitl-drive.yaml

- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `approver:`, `drive:`, `node_outputs:`, `debt_factors:`, `clock:` and `override:` blocks inside `hitl.yaml`
- After: one deployment-authored `DeclaredArtifact`, `role: load`, with `clock.base` moved to `decision.yaml`
- Why: what one run's human actors did is not a standing statement of what the fabric was permitted to do — the POLICY governing them is, and that is registered separately
- Migration: split the policy out, move the clock seed to your decision-inputs document, and read the rest off `booted.artifacts`

*Recorded by `15-17-example-06.md`.*

- After: a deployment-authored `DeclaredArtifact`, `role: load`, parsed once by `load_and_boot` and handed to the token source

- After: a deployment-authored `DeclaredArtifact`, `role: load`, carrying the boundary, the decision instant and the clock seed
- Why: what instant one run asked about is not part of what the fabric was permitted to do; the clock seed joins them because it is the same kind of value and must be readable **before** the first registration
- Migration: gather the instants into one document; read it through `load_artifact` ahead of `load_and_boot` if your audit clock is artifact-seeded

- After: a `ccf.signal-batch` in the SDK's own envelope vocabulary (`tenant_id`, `source: {system_id}`, `raw_payload`), ingested in one line

- Migration: rename the payload keys to the envelope's own and delete the translation helper

#### examples/06-decision-replay/run.py

- Surface: examples/06-decision-replay/run.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: 1,320 lines, 175 mechanical (five marshalling helpers, a 77-line operator-construction function, a 41-line registration prologue, an 8-line boot), 130 relocatable assert/print lines, and a `record()`/`replay_decision()` split whose assertions ran only under `make example-06`
- After: 1,075 lines, 12 mechanical, 0 asserts, 0 prints — one `run(*, root)` returning a typed result, with the guarantee relocated to `tests/examples/test_decision_replay_output.py`
- Why: `make examples` proves a spine runs; `pytest` proves it is correct (D-09). What stays in adopter code stays deliberately: the MaxTTL registration, `register_product_class`, the product's own lineage, `run_gates` with its independent `engine_resolved`, and — the one that matters most in this file — the explicit `pins.version_for` lookup behind every `PolicyBinding` handed to `build_replay_record`
- Migration: split `main()` from a `run()` that returns its state; move assertions to a test module; note that `run()` leaves the fabric handle OPEN because the replay leg reads through its reader

*Recorded by `15-17-example-06.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/producer-manifest.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/producer-manifest.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: absent — `producer_child.py` registered artifacts by hand in a 32-line prologue, including a loop that registered artifacts out of another artifact's body
- After: a `ccf.deployment-manifest` naming sixteen documents by `path`, `key`, `class_id` and `role`, plus the tenant and the backend kind
- Why: the set of artifacts in force is replay dimension 2, so it must be one enumerated, content-hashable document rather than a sequence of registration calls no artifact records
- Migration: write a manifest and call `load_and_boot`; the hand-written prologue plus `boot()` still works unchanged

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-manifest.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-manifest.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: absent — `run.py` built the consumer's resolution spec from a nested `federation.entity_rule` block inside `a2a.yaml` and registered the rest by hand
- After: a second `ccf.deployment-manifest` naming eight documents, including the consumer's own resolution spec, policy bundle, admitted product class and peering entitlement
- Why: the consumer is a deployment in its own right; a shared manifest would assert that one artifact set was in force for both sides, which is exactly the thing the A2A trust boundary exists because it is not true
- Migration: give each process its own manifest rather than one bundle read twice

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/resolution-spec.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/resolution-spec.yaml

- After: a `ccf.resolution-spec` carrying `semver`, `entity_rules` and `relationship_rules` and nothing else
- Why: D-03 makes the class closed-world over resolution rules only; the two attribute maps moved to the operator-parameters documents that consume them, and `tenant_id` to the manifest
- Migration: rename the file, replace both root version keys with a single `semver`, and move each displaced block to the document named below

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-resolution-spec.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-resolution-spec.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `federation.entity_rule:` block nested inside `a2a.yaml`, translated by a bespoke `_consumer_resolution_spec` helper that read a differently-nested document
- After: a `ccf.resolution-spec` at the standard nesting, loaded by the consumer's manifest like any other
- Why: a resolution spec is a resolution spec whichever deployment authors it; the nesting variant existed only because the consumer had no manifest of its own, and it cost a 19-line translation helper
- Migration: lift the rule to a document root under `entity_rules:` and name it from your manifest

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/policy-bundle.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/policy-bundle.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `policy_bundle:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, registered as a bare mapping under the long key `larkfield-estates/policy-bundle/situation-desk`
- After: a document in the open `DeclaredArtifact` envelope — `semver`, `class_id`, `declaration` — registered under the short manifest key `policy-bundle`
- Why: the SDK never invents a policy-bundle schema, but it owns the envelope, and the envelope is what gives a deployment-authored document a version lane and a checkable class identity
- Migration: wrap your bundle in `declaration:` and add `semver` and `class_id`. Both the content and the key change, so the pinned version changes

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-policy-bundle.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-policy-bundle.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: absent — the consumer registered the *producer's* `policy_bundle` block from the same `controlplane.yaml` and separately recomputed its content hash as `producer_policy_version`
- After: the consumer's own envelope document, content-identical to the producer's and registered from the consumer's manifest
- Why: the pre-rewrite `assert policy_version == producer_policy_version` compared two reads of one file. As two documents the agreement is falsifiable, and it is exactly what the trust grant's `permitted_policy_regimes` entitles
- Migration: author the regime you entitle a peer to claim as your own document rather than reading the peer's

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/operator-parameters-similarity.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/operator-parameters-similarity.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `operator_parameters.similarity:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, with `similarity_corpus_attribute_by_class` in `ontology.yaml` and `index_version` alongside the query
- After: a `ccf.operator-parameters.similarity` document carrying query, threshold, ranked classes and the corpus attribute map; the index version moves to the governance document
- Why: the corpus attribute map governs index *construction* and belongs with the artifact that declares it. The index version does not: it identifies a corpus state at a named boundary, not a parameter set

- Before: the `perspective:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`
- After: a `ccf.perspective` document
- Why: a parsed artifact of this class **is** a `Perspective` and passes straight into projection invocation with no field translation
- Migration: lift the block to a document root and add `semver`

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/product-class.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/product-class.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `product_class:` block inside `controlplane.yaml`, carrying `plan_id` and `product_ttl_seconds` and declaring no operator set
- After: a `ccf.product-class` document declaring `required_operator_keys` and `max_ttl_key`; `plan_id` and `product_ttl_seconds` move to the deployment's own governance document
- Why: `required_operator_keys` is the authored declared side of the lineage gate. Without it the projection returned the operator registry's own keys — `SituationPlan(required=operators.keys())` — and Gate 2 compared a value against itself
- Migration: add `required_operator_keys` naming the operator keys your projection depends on, and read it in your projection instead of the registry's keys

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-product-class.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-product-class.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: absent — the consumer registered its admitted product class from the producer's `product_class:` block
- After: the consumer's own `ccf.product-class` document, naming the schema it will admit and its own TTL key
- Why: a consumer that read its admission criteria off the producer's declaration would be verifying a value against itself; `permitted_schema_names` in the trust grant is built from this
- Migration: author the classes you admit rather than inheriting the peer's

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/product-governance.yaml

- After: one deployment-authored document in the `DeclaredArtifact` envelope carrying all of them

- Migration: collect the equivalents from your Control Plane bundle into a declared document

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-product-governance.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/consumer-product-governance.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: absent — the consumer registered `max_ttl_seconds` from the producer's bundle
- After: the consumer's own envelope document carrying its own ceiling
- Why: the ceiling a consumer enforces is the consumer's policy; a peer does not get to raise it by declaring a longer one
- Migration: author your own ceiling for every class you admit

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/producer-peering.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/producer-peering.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `producer:` block and parts of the `consumer:` block inside `a2a.yaml`, read by BOTH processes
- After: the producer's own envelope document, `role: register`, naming who it is on the wire, which key id it signs under, which producing agent it asserts, and where it emits
- Why: which identity a fabric emits under is a standing statement of what it was permitted to do. Split from the consumer's entitlement so the federation relationship is stated from both sides independently — one shared document would make the signature check pass for a reason unrelated to trust
- Migration: split your federation bundle along the trust boundary: what you assert goes in your Control Plane, what you entitle a peer to assert goes in theirs

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/peering-trust.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/peering-trust.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `consumer:` and `producer:` blocks inside `a2a.yaml`
- After: the consumer's own envelope document, `role: register`, carrying the trust-policy REQUEST — peer ids, the key identifier the peer may sign under, the producing agent it may assert, the ceilings, and the receive path
- Why: **this is deliberately not a `ccf.peering-trust-policy`, despite that class being real SDK mechanism.** `PeerTrustGrant` requires `signing_keys` — key MATERIAL, generated per run and never committed — so a standalone document of that class cannot be authored and a header naming it would be one its own class rejects. What is authorable is the request; `run.py` completes it with the run's public key. Same shape as `max_ttl_version`'s deliberate absence from a product-class document
- Migration: author the policy half of your trust entry and supply key material at registration time

*Recorded by `15-18-example-04.md`.*

#### examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/federation-ingest.yaml

- Surface: examples/04-cross-process/artifacts/federation-ingest.yaml
- Public surface: false
- Change type: reshaped
- Before: the `federation:` block inside `a2a.yaml`, mixing the L1 mapping with the entity resolution rule
- After: a deployment-authored `role: register` document carrying the mapping only; the entity rule moved to `consumer-resolution-spec.yaml`
- Why: D-03 makes a resolution spec closed-world over resolution rules, so the rule and the mapping cannot share a document. What shape of peer claim a fabric turns into signal is a standing permission, hence `register`
- Migration: split the block: rules to your resolution spec, aliases and the source system id to a declared document

- Why: the set of artifacts in force is replay dimension 2; a registration sequence no artifact records cannot be resolved back out of it
- Migration: write a manifest and call `load_and_boot`; keep author-versioned classes out of it and register them yourself

- Surface: examples/external/00-hello-context/run.py
- Public surface: false

- Before: 766 lines, 36 mechanical — a 22-line hand-built operator registry in `HelloReplayEnvironment`, an 8-line `boot` and its registration prologue inside the D-16 assertion, and four `fabric._load(...)` reads reaching into `serve.py`'s private helpers
- After: 781 lines, 0 mechanical — one `load_and_boot` of the same manifest at the top of `main()`, whose result supplies both the artifact values every assertion reads and the graph PASS 8 replays against; `build_operator_registry` rebuilds the operators
- Why: the replica must be rebuilt from the artifacts the original ran on, and reaching into another module's private helpers to do it was a second, drift-prone description of the same deployment. The raw line count rose 15; see the section above for why, and for why the 22 relocatable print lines could not move
- Migration: boot your replica from the same manifest; do not import another module's private loaders

## Removed

Surfaces that no longer exist. Each entry names what replaces it.

0 entries — 0 public Python surface, 0 not public Python API.

*No entries.*

## Behavior changes

Surfaces whose **signature did not move** but whose observable behavior did. A generated signature diff is structurally incapable of seeing these, which is why D-15 asks for a curated log alongside the manifest rather than instead of it.

27 entries — 19 public Python surface, 8 not public Python API.

### Public Python surface

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.load_artifact

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.load_artifact
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: parsed with `yaml.safe_load`, so PyYAML's timestamp resolver ran and a
  timestamp-shaped scalar arrived as a **naive** `datetime` — which
  `ControlPlaneRegistry.register` then refused as non-canonicalizable, forcing
  every caller to hand-normalize (six distinct helper implementations had
  drifted across eleven sites)
- After: parses through a private `_ArtifactLoader` with the
  `tag:yaml.org,2002:timestamp` resolver removed, so the scalar arrives as
  `str`; a contract field annotated `datetime` parses it into an **aware**
  value, and a date-time naming neither `Z` nor a `±HH:MM` offset is refused at
  parse by `_reject_naive_timestamps` with a message naming D-IMPL-38
- Why: D-02. `Z` is the ISO-8601 UTC designator, not an implicit timezone — the
  author stated UTC explicitly and PyYAML's resolver is what drops it. Refusing
  the author's document punished them for a rule they did not break, because the
  naive value was a *parser* defect rather than an authoring one. The SDK now
  reads timestamps as text and lets the contract parse them, exactly as it
  already refuses floats rather than repairing them. The resolver table is
  copied rather than mutated in place, so YAML parsing elsewhere in the process
  is unaffected.
- Migration: a caller relying on `load_artifact` handing back a `datetime` for a
  field its contract does not annotate as one now receives a `str` — annotate
  the field `datetime` on the contract and Pydantic parses it, aware. A document
  carrying a zone-less date-time is now refused rather than silently read as
  naive; state the zone as `Z` or as an explicit offset. No public
  timestamp-parsing helper is offered, deliberately: one would invite adopters
  to keep hand-normalizing the thing the loader now gets right.

*Recorded by `15-05-timestamps.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.registry.ControlPlaneProtocol

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.registry.ControlPlaneProtocol
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the Protocol declared `register`, `register_typed`, `resolve`,
  `resolve_latest` and `audit_events`. `register_versioned` — the D-05 hash-verified
  bump check — existed only on the reference `ControlPlaneRegistry`, so a conforming
  implementation could omit it entirely
- After: the Protocol also declares
  `register_versioned(key, artifact, artifact_class, *, actor=None, reissuable=False) -> str | RegistrationRefusal`,
  with a signature identical to the reference implementation's
- Why: D-IMPL-82 (Dave, 2026-08-05). The bump check is a governance obligation, and
  the Protocol's own docstring already argues that the obligations sit on the surface
  "precisely so a remote plane cannot conform while quietly dropping them" — a remote
  Control Plane that cannot run the bump check is exactly such a plane, and
  `load_and_boot` must be able to dispatch author-versioned classes to it to close
  CR-1 at all (T-15.1-01-01). Rejected: a narrow second
  `VersionedRegistrationProtocol`, which preserves the posture at the cost of a
  two-Protocol mental model in the phase whose subject is one mental model; and
  narrowing `load_and_boot` to the concrete `ControlPlaneRegistry`, which breaks the
  Protocol-over-inheritance posture CLAUDE.md §11 mandates. Recorded as
  `Change type: behavior` rather than `reshaped` because
  `docs/surface/v0.2-surface.json` records a Protocol's shape as `(*args, **kwargs)`:
  a member addition changes what conforms without moving any signature the generated
  manifest can see, which is the case the `behavior` class exists for
  (`docs/surface-changes/README.md`)
- Migration: a non-reference Control Plane implementation must now provide
  `register_versioned` — this is a FABR-07 remote-plane implementation obligation.
  The in-process `ControlPlaneRegistry` already satisfies it and needs no change; no
  caller's code changes. An implementation that omits the member no longer type-checks
  as conforming, and `isinstance(x, ControlPlaneProtocol)` returns `False` for it

*Recorded by `15.1-01-projection-requirements-and-protocol.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.classes.resolve_artifact_class

- Surface: ccf.deployment.classes.resolve_artifact_class
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `resolve_artifact_class(class_id)` returned the SDK constant for an id in
  `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` and the open `DeclaredArtifact` envelope for **every** other id,
  including every `ccf.`-prefixed one. The module docstring justified that with "refusing
  it would be the SDK claiming ownership of a namespace that is not its own"
- After: the name and the signature are unchanged and no manifest in the tree resolves
  differently. Two behavior changes: the six author-versioned ids now resolve to their
  **real contracts** instead of the envelope, and a `ccf.`-prefixed id in neither SDK
  mapping now raises `ValueError` naming D-IMPL-84 instead of resolving to the envelope.
  The docstring's namespace sentence is rewritten: the SDK claims exactly the `ccf.`
  prefix and nothing else, and the rejected four-id alternative is recorded with the
  reason
- Why: D-IMPL-84. The envelope path was reachable *under an SDK class name*, which is
  what made the squat work — arbitrary content content-hashed into Control Plane state,
  pinned into `ResolvedPins` and therefore into Decision Replay dimension 2, bound to
  `ccf.workflow-definition` with no `artifact_version` at all. The reservation is one
  prefix wide and no wider: every adopter namespace stays on the envelope path unchanged,
  and `tests/deployment/test_class_lookup.py` asserts that half too, so an implementation
  that refused *every* unrecognized id would fail
- Migration: **a deployment naming a `ccf.`-prefixed class id it does not own must rename
  it into its own namespace.** Concretely: a manifest entry whose `class_id` starts with
  `ccf.` and is neither in `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` nor in
  `SDK_VERSIONED_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` now fails at load with a message naming D-IMPL-84 and
  saying what to do. Two cases land there and both are the same instruction: an id that
  was a typo for a real SDK class (correct the spelling), and a deployment-authored class
  that borrowed the SDK's prefix (rename it — `acme.workflow-definition`, not
  `ccf.workflow-definition`). Nothing in this repository required the edit. Note also
  that `ccf.hitl-policy` and `ccf.scope-mapping` — SDK classes registered by adopter code
  against their real contracts, never through a manifest — are refused here rather than
  enveloped, which is a strict improvement on the hazard `examples/05-workflow-hitl`
  previously documented as something the adopter had to know to avoid

*Recorded by `15.1-03-namespace-reservation.md`.*

#### ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ManifestEntry

- Surface: ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ManifestEntry
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: one validator on `path`, rejecting only the glob characters `*`, `?` and `[`.
  An absolute path and a `..` segment both validated, so a manifest could name any file
  on the host
- After: a second validator, `_path_stays_beside_the_manifest`, refusing an absolute
  path, a rooted or drive-qualified path, and any `..` segment — asked of both
  `PurePosixPath` and `PureWindowsPath`, so one content-hashed document is admissible on
  every platform or on none. No field is added, removed or retyped
- Why: CR-01. `ManifestEntry.path`'s stated contract is "relative to the manifest file's
  own directory" and the contract did not hold it. D-IMPL-84 had already established in
  this same phase that a manifest is *not* assumed benign — the whole `ccf.` namespace is
  reserved precisely because a manifest can be authored to squat an SDK class id — and a
  document trusted that little does not also get to name `/`-rooted paths. The check is
  syntactic and therefore belongs at the boundary (CLAUDE.md §11: validation is at the
  boundary, never deferred); it is deliberately not the whole answer, because a plain
  name can still be a symlink out, which is what the `load_and_boot` entry below covers
- Migration: **a manifest naming a path outside its own directory now fails to parse**,
  with an `ArtifactLoadError` naming the offending path. No manifest in this repository
  was affected — every entry across the eleven example manifests and the deployment
  fixtures is already a plain relative name, and subdirectory-relative paths such as
  `agents/agent-definition.yaml` are unaffected because the rule is *inside the
  manifest's directory*, not *flat*. An adopter whose manifest reached outside must move
  or copy the document into the deployment, where it is versioned with the rest of the
  artifact set

*Recorded by `15.1-11-manifest-path-containment.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.ManifestEntry

- Surface: ccf.deployment.ManifestEntry
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the `ccf.deployment` re-export of the contract above, with the same
  glob-only validation
- After: the same re-export, carrying the added containment refusal
- Why: the package re-export is a distinct fully-qualified name and the phase-close
  cross-check matches at that granularity, so the behavior change is recorded against
  both paths an adopter can import. There is no separate mechanism here — it is the same
  class object
- Migration: identical to `ccf.controlplane.artifacts.ManifestEntry` above; the import
  path an adopter used does not change what is refused

*Recorded by `15.1-11-manifest-path-containment.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.manifest.load_and_boot

- Surface: ccf.deployment.manifest.load_and_boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `document = (root / entry.path).read_text(...)`, with nothing between the
  manifest's declaration and the open. Any YAML-parseable file on the host landed on
  `BootedFabric.artifacts`, and on `role: register` was content-hashed into Control Plane
  state and therefore into Decision Replay dimension 2; on a non-parseable file the
  `ArtifactLoadError` carried the file's contents back to the caller
- After: each entry's document is resolved against the manifest's own directory in
  **preflight** — before any document is opened and before anything is registered — and
  an entry resolving outside it raises `ValueError` naming the key, the declared path and
  the resolved path. `resolve()` follows symlinks, so a plain name that links out is
  refused; a link resolving back inside is admitted. The refusal names the resolved path
  so an operator can locate it and never the file's contents, which is the disclosure the
  check exists to stop
- Why: CR-01, and the reason this half exists separately from the contract half is
  measured rather than assumed — with only the contract validator in place, a symlinked
  plain name still booted and returned a file from outside the deployment
  (`tests/deployment/test_manifest.py::test_a_symlink_out_of_the_deployment_is_refused_at_the_read`).
  The preflight placement follows the ordering `_preflight_backend` already establishes
  in this function: a manifest whose seventh entry escapes must not have read its first
  six, and must certainly not have registered them
- Migration: **a manifest whose entry resolves outside its own directory now raises
  `ValueError` instead of booting.** Nothing else changes: the signature, the return
  type, the pin set and its content hash are all untouched, and every manifest already
  naming files beside itself behaves identically. A deployment relying on a symlink out
  of its own tree replaces the link with the document

*Recorded by `15.1-11-manifest-path-containment.md`.*

#### ccf.deployment.load_and_boot

- Surface: ccf.deployment.load_and_boot
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the `ccf.deployment` re-export of the function above, with no containment check
- After: the same re-export, carrying the preflight containment refusal
- Why: the package re-export is a distinct fully-qualified name and is the import path
  the examples and the deployment tests actually use (`from ccf.deployment import
  load_and_boot`), so it is the one most adopters will read. Recorded against both for
  the same name-granularity reason as `ManifestEntry` above
- Migration: identical to `ccf.deployment.manifest.load_and_boot` above

*Recorded by `15.1-11-manifest-path-containment.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.protocols.GraphReader.query

- Surface: ccf.graph.protocols.GraphReader.query
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: absent — the only question the read seam could carry was
  `as_of(tenant_id, at)`; a predicate-bearing question had no wire into any
  reader
- After: `query(self, q: GraphQuery) -> GraphView` on the Protocol and on
  every conformer in the tree. Tenant scope arrives inside the frozen value
  (D-IMPL-05 satisfied by the contract, `GQ-01`); a conformant answerer
  answers any well-formed query, degrading internally to scan-and-filter
  where it holds no native translation (`GQ-29`, D-04); an empty answer is
  an answer, never an error (`GQ-23`)
- Why: D-04 rules that adapters always answer — no capability negotiation,
  no typed pushdown-refusal path — so the seam must exist on the Protocol
  before any pushdown translation does, and sqlite/postgres must answer any
  GraphQuery (including `allowed_entities`-bearing ones) from this plan on
- Migration: external `GraphReader` implementers must implement `query()`
  (and `attestation`, below). The reference degrade path is available to
  them: materialize records, then apply `ccf.graph.fallback.answer` for the
  query's predicate families and `allowed_entities`, then order per `GQ-22`.
  `as_of()` MAY be derived as
  `self.query(GraphQuery(tenant_id=tenant_id, as_of=at))` (D-07, `GQ-21`)

*Recorded by `15.2-05-query-seam-open.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.protocols.GraphReader.attestation

- Surface: ccf.graph.protocols.GraphReader.attestation
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: absent — a reader had no typed way to state how its answers
  acquired policy binding and lineage
- After: a property returning `SourceAttestation` (SDK-defined per CLAUDE.md
  §11). The live graph reports tier 1 (`assembly-stamped` /
  `graph-supplied` / `peer=None`); `SnapshotReader` delegates to the wrapped
  reader — a pin narrows scope, not provenance
- Why: the D-09 ruling makes the three trust tiers three well-known values
  of one schema; the seam-level property is what lets the engine read the
  attestation in force and pin it into replay with zero new mechanism
- Migration: external implementers return their source's modes; a live-graph
  wrapper returns the tier-1 value

*Recorded by `15.2-05-query-seam-open.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.reader.BaseGraphReader

- Surface: ccf.graph.reader.BaseGraphReader
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `as_of()` acquired rows via the two `SELECT_*_AS_OF` statements
  and materialized them; no predicate-bearing question existed
- After: `query(q)` is the template method — the `_translate_query(q)` hook
  answers the whole query or returns `None` (`GQ-32`; safe narrowing under a
  declined translation per `GQ-33`; no partial-family bookkeeping exists);
  the baseline path acquires tenant+as-of rows, materializes through the
  15.2-03 substrate-neutral core, applies `ccf.graph.fallback.answer`, and
  freezes through the interface-owned ordering. `as_of()` is now
  `self.query(GraphQuery(tenant_id=tenant_id, as_of=at))` — the D-07
  derivation, byte-identical, zero call-site churn. `attestation` reports
  tier 1. `translated_answers` / `fallback_answers` are per-instance
  monotonic telemetry counters, deliberately outside the replay record
  (D-04 — performance is never a replay input)
- Why: adapters supply dialect and rows; everything above the rows —
  materialization, semantics, ordering — must exist once, or the
  differential suite would be comparing accidents
- Migration: SQL backend subclasses (sqlite, postgres) inherit everything;
  no subclass change. A subclass MAY override `_translate_query` to earn
  native pushdown, under the whole-query-or-None contract

*Recorded by `15.2-05-query-seam-open.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.snapshots.SnapshotReader

- Surface: ccf.replay.snapshots.SnapshotReader.query
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the pinned reader scope-checked `as_of` / history reads only; a
  `GraphQuery` had no path into replay
- After: `query(q)` scope-checks `q.tenant_id` / `q.as_of` against the
  pinned snapshot before delegating to the wrapped reader's `query()`,
  raising `SnapshotScopeViolation` on mismatch — the deliberate
  raise-vs-empty-view contrast of `GQ-27`: a source answers questions; a
  pin enforces one. `attestation` delegates to the wrapped reader. The
  `as_of` delegation path is byte-identical to before
- Why: replayed executions will phrase reads as queries from plan 15.2-08
  on; a replay that could query outside its pin would silently import the
  future into a record of the past
- Migration: none — existing replay callers are unchanged

*Recorded by `15.2-05-query-seam-open.md`.*

#### ccf.operators.categories.PolicyFilter.read_intent

- Surface: ccf.operators.categories.PolicyFilter.read_intent
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: absent — the policy operator had no declared substrate rendering
- After: returns the policy-family `QueryFragment`
  (`permitted_policy_bundle_versions`, `classification_attribute_by_class`,
  `permitted_classifications`), a field-for-field projection of its
  parameter contract (`GQ-02`/`GQ-08`)
- Why: D-03 — the pushed admissibility predicate must mirror `_admits`
  exactly; the per-operator equivalence property in
  `tests/conformance/test_substrate_properties.py` pins the mirror
- Migration: none — `apply()` is byte-identical

*Recorded by `15.2-07-read-intent-segmentation.md`.*

#### ccf.operators.categories.TemporalAsOf.read_intent

- Surface: ccf.operators.categories.TemporalAsOf.read_intent
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: absent
- After: returns the temporal-family `QueryFragment`
  (`require_valid_at_boundary`); the as-of cut itself rides the mandatory
  `GraphQuery.as_of` (`GQ-07`/`GQ-09`)
- Why: D-03, as above

- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: absent
- After: returns a traverse-family `QueryFragment` carrying a
  `TraversalSpec` built from `seed_entity_classes` / `relationship_types` /
  `depth_limit` (`GQ-10`); never seeds, never `allowed_entities` (`GQ-06`)

#### ccf.operators.categories.SimilaritySelect.read_intent

- Surface: ccf.operators.categories.SimilaritySelect.read_intent
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: absent
- After: returns `None`, full stop — Similarity consults an injected
  `SimilarityIndexProtocol` the substrate does not hold and is never
  pushable (D-03; the Phase 15.2 Deferred-Ideas ruling)
- Why: making Similarity pushable is a decision reversal, not an
  optimization; the pushability-matrix pin fails a drive-by change by name
- Migration: none

#### ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: dimensions 2 and 5 carried no query pins and no cap value
- After: signature unchanged — the new facts arrive on `execution` itself:
  `execution.executed_queries` becomes the dimension-5 pin tuple (hashed
  here, in order) and `execution.survivor_cap` becomes the dimension-2 cap
  pin
- Why: execution facts already travel to the builder on `ExecutionResult`;
  a new parameter would have been a second channel for the same facts
- Migration: none — executions built without the fields yield both sentinels

*Recorded by `15.2-08-replay-query-pins.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.engine.replay

- Surface: ccf.replay.engine.replay
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: re-execution ran with no survivor cap and compared the step
  sequence, the snapshot id, and the product address
- After: re-execution runs under the **recorded** dimension-2
  `survivor_cap`, and after the step-sequence check the re-executed query
  pins — one `content_hash` per issued `GraphQuery`, in order — are compared
  against the recorded `graph_query_sequence`; a mismatch is
  `ReplayDivergence`. A record carrying the pre-seam `NO_GRAPH_QUERIES`
  sentinel asserts it predates the query seam and the pin comparison stands
  down — the snapshot and product checks still bind it to the same answer
- Why: D-11 — replay recomputes each question and compares hashes, so a
  doctored pin tuple or a drifted question sequence is caught non-determinism;
  D-10 — the split depends on the cap, so re-executing under the deployment's
  *current* cap would be a re-computation wearing the decision's identifier
- Migration: none — pre-phase records replay exactly as before

*Recorded by `15.2-08-replay-query-pins.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.engine.resolve_artifact

- Surface: ccf.replay.engine.resolve_artifact
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: fetched bytes were validated with the **current** record class and
  the rebuilt model was re-hashed as the tamper check — so
  sentinel-defaulted fields added by later contract versions changed the
  re-derived hash, and honest pre-extension records raised
  `ReplayDivergence` (the false A5 assumption, empirically reproduced on
  `PRE_CHANGE_CANONICAL`: stored `26cdaded…`, re-derived `ad21e6ab…`)
- After: verifies `sha256(raw fetched bytes) == content_address` **before**
  model validation; the re-validated-model re-hash is gone
- Why: D-IMPL-89 (option a) — the put-time address *is* the sha256 of the
  stored canonical bytes and the store's `get` contract returns those exact
  bytes, so byte fidelity carries identical tamper-detection strength
  (T-04-17) while being model-class-independent — which is what lets every
  sentinel-default record extension (I.2, I.3, I.4, 15.2-08) keep its
  compatibility promise
- Migration: none — every artifact a conforming store holds verifies as
  before; pre-extension records additionally verify again

*Recorded by `15.2-08-replay-query-pins.md`.*

#### ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record

- Surface: ccf.replay.record.build_replay_record
- Public surface: true
- Change type: behavior
- Before: dimension 1 carried no attestation
- After: signature unchanged — the new fact arrives on `execution` itself:
  `execution.source_attestation` becomes dimension 1's
  `source_attestation`, exactly as the 15.2-08 facts arrived
- Why: execution facts already travel to the builder on `ExecutionResult`;
  a new parameter would have been a second channel for the same fact
- Migration: none — executions built without the field yield the `None`
  sentinel

*Recorded by `15.2-11-attestation-enforcement.md`.*

### Not public Python API

#### tools/check_schema_headers.py

- Surface: tools/check_schema_headers.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: a header naming anything outside `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES` was rejected, so deployment-authored documents were unannotatable
- After: a header naming `ccf.declared-artifact` is accepted when the envelope contract accepts the document, the document declares a `class_id`, and that class is not one the SDK owns; the manifest cross-check then compares the entry's `class_id` against the *document's* field rather than the header
- Why: the indirection must not become an escape hatch. An SDK-owned class pointed at the open envelope would silence every constraint it declares, so that is refused, and the two-independent-statements property the cross-check exists for is preserved rather than dropped
- Migration: none — existing headers are unaffected

*Recorded by `15-13-example-00.md`.*

#### tools/check_schema_headers.py

- Surface: tools/check_schema_headers.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: a `$schema` header could only name a member of `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`, and the envelope route refused only class ids in that same set
- After: both the header lookup and the envelope route's refusal read the union of the manifest-addressable and author-versioned sets
- Why: without the second half, an agent definition could annotate itself as deployment-authored — its class id is absent from `SDK_ARTIFACT_CLASSES`, which used to be the whole test — and have every constraint `AgentDefinition` declares go unchecked
- Migration: none — existing headers are unaffected

*Recorded by `15-14-examples-01-02.md`.*

#### tests/examples/test_ext00_hardening.py

- Surface: tests/examples/test_ext00_hardening.py
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: two private loader helpers read `tools` and `tool_allowlist` from the root of `experience.yaml`
- After: they read them from under `declaration:`, following the document onto the `DeclaredArtifact` envelope
- Why: unavoidable consequence of the reshape this plan mandates — the envelope route is the only honest `$schema` header available to a deployment-authored class. No assertion in the module changed; only where the two loaders index
- Migration: n/a

*Recorded by `15-20-external.md`.*

#### ccf.graph._writer.MemoryGraphWriter

- Surface: ccf.graph._writer.MemoryGraphWriter
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the typed write seam (`_GraphTxn`, plan 15.2-02) had only SQL
  implementations — `_SqlGraphTxn` driving dialect statements for SQLite
  and Postgres
- After: `_MemoryTxn` implements the nine typed operations natively over
  the store's per-tenant dicts — the first non-SQL proof of the seam —
  honouring the rowcount contract (0 for an ignored duplicate claim id, the
  writer's idempotency signal) and all-or-nothing transactions via lazy
  per-tenant snapshot with in-place rollback. `MemoryGraphWriter` inherits
  the whole `BaseGraphWriter` algorithm with no SQL ClassVars. Both live in
  `ccf.graph._writer` only (CINV-02 — write code outside that module is
  write code outside the guard)
- Why: D-05/D-06 lock that conformance writes go through booted L2, and a
  dict store cannot execute SQL text; the typed seam exists precisely so
  this class could be written without interpreting SQL constants
- Migration: none — the module is import-restricted to `ccf.assembly` /
  `ccf.boot`; deployments reach the writer only through
  `boot(memory=True)`

*Recorded by `15.2-06-memory-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph._writer.Neo4jGraphWriter

- Surface: ccf.graph._writer.Neo4jGraphWriter
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the typed write seam (`_GraphTxn`, plan 15.2-02) had one non-SQL
  implementation (`_MemoryTxn`, plan 15.2-06) beside the shared SQL
  adapter
- After: `_Neo4jTxn` implements the nine typed operations as parameterized
  Cypher over one explicit driver transaction — `MERGE` on the compound
  identity honours the rowcount contract (`nodes_created` is 0 for an
  ignored duplicate claim id, the writer's idempotency signal; for lineage
  the L-axis set-union itself, INV-CH7-17), reads inside the transaction
  see its own writes, and a per-partition `CcfMeta` counter renders the
  insertion-order tiebreak (`rowid`/`BIGSERIAL`). Write-time projections
  (`_neo4j_claim_projection`) store the predicate-relevant claim fields —
  validity window re-rendered through `canonical_timestamp`,
  `entity_class`, string-valued attributes under a prefixed key — so the
  read path needs no JSON parsing in Cypher. `Neo4jGraphWriter` inherits
  the whole `BaseGraphWriter` algorithm with no SQL ClassVars. All of it
  lives in `ccf.graph._writer` only (CINV-02; threat T-15.2-06 — no
  session or transaction construction exists in the reader module beyond
  reads)
- Why: D-05/D-06 lock that conformance writes go through booted L2, and a
  Cypher store cannot execute SQL text; the typed seam exists precisely so
  this class could be written without interpreting SQL constants
- Migration: none — the module is import-restricted to `ccf.assembly` /
  `ccf.boot`; deployments reach the writer only through
  `boot(neo4j_uri=…)`

*Recorded by `15.2-12-neo4j-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph._writer.MongoGraphWriter

- Surface: ccf.graph._writer.MongoGraphWriter
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: the typed write seam (`_GraphTxn`, plan 15.2-02) had two non-SQL
  implementations — `_MemoryTxn` (15.2-06) and `_Neo4jTxn` (15.2-12) — beside
  the shared SQL adapter, and every one of them held an open substrate
  transaction
- After: `_MongoTxn` implements the nine typed operations as **buffered
  pymongo bulk operations** — the first *batched* implementation of the seam,
  and the case the `_GraphTxn` contract names in as many words (D-IMPL-88):
  mutating operations resolve their rowcounts before commit from committed
  state read before buffering plus the buffer's own contents (sound because
  INV-CH7-10 guarantees no concurrent writer), and `select_*_chain` merges the
  committed chain with the claim appended earlier in the same transaction.
  Buffering rather than a MongoDB multi-document transaction is deliberate: a
  plain `mongo:7` deployment is a standalone server and multi-document
  transactions require a replica set, which a GitHub Actions service container
  cannot be asked to start — so the adapter's conformance does not depend on a
  topology. Ordering inside a collection is preserved by
  `bulk_write(ordered=True)`, which is what lets the chain restitch update a
  claim the same transaction inserted; every operation is an upsert on its
  compound identity, so the writer's whole algorithm stays idempotent
  (INV-CH7-04). The doubled-edge traversal documents are maintained here, in
  the same buffered transaction as the relationship node they double, so the
  layout cannot drift from the edge it represents. Write-time projections
  (`_mongo_claim_projection`) store the predicate-relevant claim fields —
  validity window re-rendered through `canonical_timestamp`, `entity_class`,
  string-valued attributes whose names `attribute_field_path` admits as
  document field paths. All of it lives in `ccf.graph._writer` only (CINV-02;
  threat T-15.2-06 — no write operation exists in the reader module)
- Why: D-05/D-06 lock that conformance writes go through booted L2, and a
  document store executes neither SQL nor Cypher; the typed seam exists
  precisely so this class could be written without interpreting SQL constants
- Migration: none — the module is import-restricted to `ccf.assembly` /
  `ccf.boot`; deployments reach the writer only through `boot(mongo_uri=…)`

*Recorded by `15.2-13-mongo-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph._writer._FlureeTxn / FlureeGraphWriter

- Surface: ccf.graph._writer._FlureeTxn / FlureeGraphWriter
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: five `_GraphTxn` implementations existed — one shared SQL adapter, a
  dict-backed transaction, a Cypher-backed one and a buffered pymongo one
- After: a sixth, and the first whose commit is a **single HTTP POST**.
  `_FlureeTxn` buffers the nine typed operations as ground JSON-LD triples and
  commits one `delete` + `insert` body with **no `where` clause** — a shape
  probed before it was written (F-A4(a)) and ruled `approved` by Dave on
  2026-08-08, so D-02's letter holds and the SPARQL UPDATE multi-operation
  departure is not taken. Per-operation rowcounts resolve from committed-state
  reads plus the transaction's own buffer, exactly as `_MongoTxn` resolves them;
  no receipt field is consulted, because `flakes_added` does not exist on this
  build and could never have implemented a per-operation contract anyway.
  Supersession and observed-window widening are ground delete/insert pairs
  rather than overwrites, because RDF set semantics makes a bare re-assertion
  *add* a value beside the existing one instead of replacing it — the one place
  `_MongoTxn` is not a complete template. `_fluree_claim_projection` re-renders
  both validity bounds through `parse_timestamp` → `canonical_timestamp`
  (DI-15.2-03 non-reintroduction) and materializes a multi-valued
  `ccf_policy_key`, because this engine's dynamic property access `c[$key]`
  parses, returns `200` and evaluates to **null** — porting the Neo4j policy
  predicate verbatim would have excluded every claim while reporting success
- Why: this is the SUBQ-13 content. `_GraphTxn` states its contract in terms of
  *observable results* rather than mechanism, which is why one Protocol covers a
  held Postgres transaction, a buffered bulk write and a single JSON-LD POST.
  Landing the sixth backend without touching the Protocol — 864 lines added to
  `ccf.graph._writer`, zero deleted — is D-IMPL-88's hedge paying off, measured
  rather than narrated
- Migration: none — `ccf.graph._writer` is import-restricted to `ccf.assembly`
  and `ccf.boot` by CINV-02, so no adopter can hold a reference to either name.
  The write path is reached only through booted L2

*Recorded by `15.3-02-fluree-backend.md`.*

#### ccf.graph.backends.fluree.policy_key

- Surface: ccf.graph.backends.fluree.policy_key
- Public surface: false
- Change type: behavior
- Before: `policy_key` was the *read* side's key construction only. The write
  side held its own copy of the separator (`_POLICY_KEY_SEPARATOR` in
  `ccf.graph._writer`) and joined the `(entity_class, attribute, value)` triple
  by hand, on the stated argument that importing the reader from the writer
  would break the CINV-02 confinement and create a cycle. The argument was
  false — the write path already imported the reader — and the duplicate is
  what made defect CR-01 reachable, because the injectivity guard lived on one
  copy only
- After: the duplicate constant and the hand-rolled join are deleted.
  `_fluree_claim_projection` calls `policy_key` and skips the triples it
  refuses, so a key that cannot be built on the read side is no longer stored
  on the write side. The observable answer does not move: an unencodable stored
  key was already unreachable from the read path (every pushed key carries
  exactly two separators; a smuggled one carries more), and a query naming such
  a triple still declines the pushdown so the fallback answers the whole thing
  (D-04). What moves is that the over-disclosure surface is now closed at both
  ends rather than at one
- Why: D-14 (Dave, 2026-08-09), review finding WR-01. Relocating a
  known-defective duplicate unchanged is worse than either fixing it or
  deferring it explicitly, and the writer split removes the last shred of the
  duplicate's justification
- Migration: none. `policy_key`'s own signature and behaviour are untouched;
  only its second caller is new

*Recorded by `15.4-03-write-modules.md`.*
